2026-11-12 Shared current-profile Program ZIP contract (source only; no rebuild/install/ZIP):

- The generic capture reads the current saved revision, stable source identities or unique exact execution matches, active source records, entry scripts, all companion files, field defaults/behaviors, commands/events/page declarations, and literal skin families. Unknown future Button/record fields, missing controls/panels, ambiguous identities, cross-owner execution, unsupported asset references and local/catalog drift fail explicitly. Empty installed panels are retained. Byte-identical plain scripts receive staging-only manifest wrappers preserving their installed command data; portable Windows wrappers retain program '*'. Every occurrence, skin section/variant, state cycle, field geometry, surface policy and arrangement is checked. Staged catalog files must match the verified source bytes, including future untracked helpers that Git copying would otherwise omit. Input hashes and directory inventories are checked again for changes during capture. Skin image/font references must be embedded data or local SVG fragments; unresolved relative/absolute/remote assets stop packaging instead of silently falling back.

Generated profile wrappers participate in catalog-baseline.json. Missing/extra/changed files and private/runtime paths fail; size limits match import: 512 MiB ZIP, 1024 MiB expanded payload, 128 MiB/file, 25000 archive entries, 16 MiB inventory. Older ZIPs without inventories remain importable but do not gain the new integrity guarantee.

2026-11-11 Raised-button shadow cleanup and compact program setup in v0.1.7:
- The ghost visible behind released/raised Chipped Concrete Buttons is a cast shadow baked into their sprite artwork. The shared travel renderer now removes low-opacity cast-shadow pixels only on raised/hover travel frames; flush and pressed frames bypass the filter and retain their original pixels. The label, solid concrete RGB, geometry, skin sources, hit shapes and 480 ms full-travel timing remain unchanged.
- The alpha transfer applies only to the Chipped Concrete data-anim="cc" sprite. FirstGlass uses different sprite markers and is unaffected. Inspection of 396 frames across the 33 bundled variants found sampled outer shadows at a maximum alpha of 189/255; the filter removes alpha <= 75% while retaining opaque concrete. Runtime state switches use the existing discrete Web Animations frame stops, with no new polling.
- Test suites and UI automation remain skipped at the user's request. The normal release rebuild/restart remains required for the shared renderer change.
- Version 0.1.6 -> 0.1.7 packages this shared renderer change in Core. It applies to existing Chipped Concrete sprites without reimporting their skins; this update does not alter Main Button sizing or layout defaults.
- Expanded Add Program panels now show a compact responsive grid containing only each item's checkbox and name, with its description as a tooltip on the name. Per-item destination Panel selectors and their state are removed; items install into their package-declared panels. The manual "I confirm this EXE" checkbox and Install gate are removed. Install performs the existing native executable/preflight checks and reports failures through the setup status.

2026-11-09 New panels and programs center in their rail slot; Theme Editor Apply no longer bloats button-state.json:
- Off-center cause: a new panel owner or program Button gets document.settings.defaultSkinId (the Chipped Concrete family default) and a rect as wide as its whole slot (131.57 px). ButtonHost swaps in the family member for the label, which is narrower, and Proportional fitting pinned it to the rect's top-left, so "Testing" on the Windows panel rail sat at the slot's left edge.
- Fix: when a skin family swaps a member in (skin !== assignedSkin), ButtonHost passes centerSkinInRect and ButtonSkinRenderer's applySkinRootScale centers the uniformly scaled core in the rect (both axes). Buttons drawn with their own assigned key are unchanged. Covers adding panels and programs and renaming Buttons. No state change was needed for "Testing".
- Apply bloat cause: the Theme Editor's Apply ran embedLocalButtonSkinFamilies (meant for export) before capturing, copying each skin's whole local family into every distinct skin ("skin-N" with buttonSkinEmbeddedVariants); one Main Apply went from 6.5 to 44 MB. Fix: Apply now calls captureCompleteTheme({ localApply: true }), which skips the embedding and passes capturePortableTheme(..., keepLocalSkinMetadata: true) so skins keep their local metadata and their own ids; applyThemeFile then reuses the exact existing skins, and a newly assigned family skin keeps its local family link. Save Theme and Export still embed.
- Validation: npx tsc --noEmit clean; full Button suite 668/668 (one source-pattern test updated for the new effect dependency; new portableTheme tests for skipped source-less Buttons and in-place Apply reuse); on a copy of the live state the in-place Apply added 0 skins and changed nothing.
- Installed: FlowCell 0.1.4 (HEAD 1f01db2 + these uncommitted changes) deployed exe-only with diagnostics/centering-apply-fix-20261109/deploy.ps1 (backup, protected files unchanged, bootstrap passed). Over CDP in the real app: the five Windows panel Buttons including the new "Testing" share one center (spread 0.02 px); the real Theme Editor Apply reported "Applied theme to 28 Button occurrences." with 0 skins added (358 -> 358) and the file 6,846,423 -> 6,847,907 bytes; the program selection was restored to Illustrator. Relaunched from the Desktop shortcut. The repo release exe was rebuilt plain and the build stamps refreshed.

2026-11-09 Theme Editor Apply/Save no longer fails on panel Buttons without a source:
- Cause: the Main theme scope includes every "panel" surface Button without a program identity. The two Windows / Test sample Buttons (Concrete Moss, Chipped Concrete; script-made, no source, no action) fell in it, and capturePortableTheme (used by the Theme Editor's Apply and Save Theme) required an installed source identity for them: "'Concrete Moss' has 0 installed source identities; resolve its source before saving a portable theme."
- Fix (FlowCellFrontend/src/theme/portableTheme.ts): canBePortableThemeButton leaves Buttons with no source, action, tool-set parent, panel-owner reference or Main control out of a portable theme instead of throwing; applying the theme leaves them as they are. Buttons that have a source still need exactly one installed identity. Program themes are unaffected (every Button in their scope has an identity). Regression test in tests/portableTheme.test.mjs.
- Validation: npx tsc --noEmit clean; portableTheme, themeEditor and themeModelBehavior tests 65/65; on a copy of the live state the Main capture, import plan (no issues) and apply all succeed.
- Installed: built FlowCell 0.1.4 (HEAD 1f01db2 + this uncommitted fix; the installed exe was 0.1.3), deployed exe-only with diagnostics/theme-apply-fix-20261109/deploy.ps1 (backup, protected-file hashes unchanged, bootstrap passed) and relaunched from the Desktop shortcut. Over CDP the real Main "Theme" Button opened the Theme Editor and its Apply reported "Applied theme to 28 Button occurrences."
- Found by that check: the editor's Apply re-materializes skins through the portable path, which embeds each skin's whole local family (33 Chipped Concrete files) into every distinct key skin and drops their names. One Apply grew button-state.json from 6.5 to 44 MB (14 skins x 2.8 MB) with no visible change. That state was undone the same way as Glass Concrete (revision 1457, 6.5 MB again; replaced state in backups\glass-concrete-undo-20261110T020605Z). Opening a saved theme from the library (plain theme files) does not embed. Fixed the same day (entry above).

2026-11-09 Lithophane physical thickness and Blender STL compatibility:
- Lithophane 1.1.4 replaces raw scene-unit depth with editable physical minimum/maximum thickness, defaulting to the user's explicit 0.1/17.0 mm range. Dark pixels use the minimum and light pixels reach the maximum, preserving the original positive brightness direction. The image picker exposes both controls; optional bridge payload values support the same range, while existing Illustrator requests receive the defaults. Validation requires finite positive values and minimum <= maximum before image creation. Equal values create a uniform sheet without attempting to apply a disabled zero-strength Displace modifier. Alpha cutouts, DPI/explicit X/Y size, collision-safe names, and existing subdivision density are retained.
- Real Blender 5.1.2 geometry checks passed nineteen reduced-density cases, including the final dark 0.1 mm / light 17 mm endpoints at scene scales 1 and 0.001, alpha holes at both scales, custom ranges, uniform sheets, and negative/overbright HDR samples clamped to the requested range. All meshes were closed/manifold with correct physical X/Y/Z. The final production-density white alpha-hole mesh passed at 2,441,472 vertices/faces and 20 x 10 x 17 mm, with zero non-manifold edges and the hole preserved. Native picker properties registered with the final 0.1/17 defaults; all 13 focused unit tests passed.
- STL export now inspects the actual bpy.ops.wm.stl_export RNA and passes evaluation_mode=DAG_EVAL_VIEWPORT only when supported; Blender 5.1 does not expose that parameter. Native Blender 5.1.2 validation passed six cases across scene scales 1/0.001, edit-mode selection restoration, render-hidden objects/collections, and rejection of zero-triangle meshes.

2026-11-07 Installer runtime release before upgrade:
- A real upgrade briefly failed to overwrite AutoHotkey64.exe; after Windows released the stopped process's runtime, Retry succeeded without manually stopping any process. Installer shutdown now waits for the exact owned backend and verifies exclusive nontruncating runtime access before replacement. Ownership is rechecked while retaining its process handle to avoid PID reuse; persistent exit or file-lock failures stop setup safely instead of copying onward. Session, executable, first backend-script argument and exact headless token constrain ownership; unrelated AutoHotkey processes and saved preferences are preserved.
- Preinstall embeds and runs the new installer's current stop helper from its temporary plugin directory, so upgrades from the older no-wait helper receive the fix immediately. Preinstall and preuninstall check helper failure and abort before changing application files. Fresh empty installation skips shutdown; an existing unmarked runtime is refused without terminating it. Runtime release explicitly classifies the underlying exception in one catch: repeated typed catches in Windows PowerShell 5.1 can misclassify a sharing violation as a missing directory. Only an openable or genuinely absent runtime permits replacement. Helper ownership/long-retry tests and 13 real NSIS fixture checks passed, including persistent-lock abort before copy, retry after unlock, byte preservation and refusal of unmarked roots.

2026-11-04 Toggle Monitors verifies program-window restoration:
- The affected Claude and ChatGPT/Codex windows had valid normal-window records on the user's intended lower 1920 x 1080 XP-Pen. The old restorer counted accepted asynchronous requests as restored windows without reading their resulting bounds. A mocked reproduction queued 2 requests, performed 0 post-request rectangle checks and reported 1 restoration while the window remained unmoved.
- Normal-to-normal restoration now uses the saved physical screen rectangle directly, checks actual bounds every 300 ms, retries mismatches at 900 ms intervals and requires 900 ms of matching placement within one shared 10000 ms batch deadline. Every readback/retry rechecks the living window identity, including a post-read lifetime check. Earlier matching windows remain observed until the batch finishes. Minimized/maximized state and non-activation are preserved; visible fallback branches also check destination and size, while unsupported saved-minimized/current-normal cases skip explicitly. Secondary taskbars, malformed physical sizes and unavailable normal-window destination anchors are skipped. Logs separate verified, skipped and failed results and include executable names and expected/observed bounds.
- Per-side failedRestores metadata retains an unresolved intended placement only while the same living window is still at exactly its last observed failed frame and show state. Subsequent changed frames are learned normally. Capture returns the actual outgoing geometry for emergency rollback even when persistence retains an older unresolved target. Closed/recreated windows never inherit that target.

Focused tests increased 77 -> 92; all 92 passed in 0.862 s, with delayed/ignored requests, late displacement, lifetime/state changes, minimized/maximized cases and failure-cache protection mocked. - Native activation changes from 1 immediate verification plus a fixed 400 ms window-restore delay to 300 ms polling, 900 ms of matching monitor identities and stable usable geometry, and a 10000 ms readiness deadline. Saved/selected target positions, sizes and rotations must match before their snapshot can be overwritten. Rollback requires the original geometry before restoring source windows. Timeout diagnostics include expected, observed, missing and extra identities and elapsed milliseconds. Disconnected-subset support remains intact.
- The engine publishes an atomic transition marker before changing displays and after verified completion/rollback. A bounded read-only native command reports that marker, mutex state and runtime identity. FlowCell freezes layout learning and restore requests during a switch, waits for the completed physical rectangle set plus its existing 900 ms stability window, and retains the original outgoing geometry through intermediate arrangements. Failed/abandoned transitions do not infer success from a timer. Installed/development saved layouts are separated by runtime root; transient geometry and acknowledgements also include process ID. Legacy saved data is copied on first use and is not deleted or rewritten.

2026-11-02 Skins can carry rendered images; Concrete Moss test button, deployed to the installed FlowCell at the user's request:
- The skin validator now accepts CSS url() values that embed a base64 PNG, JPEG, WebP or AVIF image (data:image/...;base64,...) as well as fragment references. File paths, web addresses, SVG data URLs and other data types are still rejected, so a skin still never reaches a file or the network. The existing 2 MiB skin limit is unchanged. The new regression in tests/buttonSystem.test.mjs covers the allowed forms and the rejected forms. docs/ai-skills.md and the skin-author skill describe the rule.
- Repo-local state only (flowcellbackend/local, used by the FlowCell Development window): Windows / Test gained a no-action test Button named "Concrete Moss" (executionTarget null, 72 x 69.5 at 112,16, Proportional), with the new "Concrete Moss" skin as both its default and its placement skin. 2026-10-24 Illustrator Send SVG to Fusion:
- Illustrator Files gains `Send SVG to Fusion` (bundled `illustrator.send-svg-to-fusion` 1.0.0, depends on `illustrator.send-svg-to-blender`). Like `Ill Orca` it is a thin wrapper: it resolves the single active Send SVG owner through the generic program manifest and active records, refuses an owner older than Send SVG 1.3.0 (no `Send-SVG-To-Fusion.vbs` beside its source, which would otherwise silently send to Blender), and evaluates that installed export with `FLOWCELL_SEND_SVG_TARGET = "fusion"`. The selection grouping, temporary-copy cleanup, Pathfinder Unite, SVG 1.0 export, leading-number thickness, physical size, and relative placement are the same code as Send SVG to Blender.
- Send SVG to Blender 1.3.0 reads `FLOWCELL_SEND_SVG_TARGET` (default Blender; only `fusion` switches). The target picks the request/status/handoff files (`send-svg-to-fusion.*`, `Send-SVG-To-Fusion.vbs`) and the app name in messages; Ill Orca's `orca` post action applies to the Blender target only. The package now also carries `Send-SVG-To-Fusion.vbs`/`.ps1`.
- The Fusion handoff registers `Programs/Fusion 360/Fusion Git Scripts/Files/Import Illustrator SVG/import_illustrator_svg.py` as the internal Fusion action `flowcell_button_internal-illustrator-svg-import` through the normal `Install-FusionFlowCellButtons.ps1` (panel `Internal`, no panel record), targets the newest live Fusion360 PID whose bridge status is ready, launches Fusion through FusionLauncher.exe only when Fusion is closed (180 s for the bridge, plus a 110 s import deadline, inside Illustrator's 300 s wait), publishes `request.json` atomically with retries, and reads bridge status/response files with delete sharing so the add-in's `os.replace` never fails. Any post action is rejected.
- The Fusion importer works like Fusion's Insert SVG: each layer becomes one sketch named after the layer on the active component's XY plane (the root of a Part Design, which allows no extra components) and one New Body extrude of its filled regions, base on the XY plane, X/Y center at the layer offset. Fusion reads SVG px at 96/in while Illustrator writes 72/in, and imported SVG curves are fixed (Sketch.move silently does nothing), so each file is imported once to measure Fusion's native size, then re-imported with importSVG's exact `scale * p + position` at the physical size and center; placement is validated to 1e-4 of the span. Profiles are filled by even-odd nesting depth so holes stay open and islands in holes are kept; disconnected islands become bodies `<layer> 1..N` (Fusion keeps disjoint lumps separate even after Combine). Each body must be solid and span exactly 0..thickness in Z. A failure deletes the batch's extrudes, leftover direct-modeling bodies, and sketches newest-first (and closes a design the action opened). Success groups the batch as `Illustrator SVG` in a parametric timeline and leaves the new bodies selected; with no open design, a new one is created.
- Changed: Illustrator `flowcell.program.json`, README, Send SVG to Blender `18_Prepare_Selected_OBJ_Export.jsx` + tests, new `Send-SVG-To-Fusion.vbs`/`.ps1`, new `Send SVG to Fusion/` package + tests, Ill Orca test (pins Send SVG 1.3.0), new Fusion `Import Illustrator SVG/` package + tests, Fusion README, and this summary. No FlowCell frontend/Rust, Fusion add-in, or Blender code changed, so no FlowCell rebuild or Fusion/Blender restart is required.
- Validation: 51 Illustrator node tests (Send SVG, Ill Orca, Send SVG to Fusion; including Windows PowerShell atomic status/request writes) and 10 Fusion importer unit tests pass; the Fusion package tests still pass. Live on this PC (Fusion Personal, Illustrator 30.8.2): probes established the 96/72 px scale, unmirrored orientation, fixed curves, and `scale * p + position`; the importer reproduced ring/hole/island, triangle, and Bezier-circle geometry exactly; a failing second layer rolled the whole batch back; the handoff script ran in about 1 s and reported Ok/Error status; and a real Illustrator export (compound ring with island, live text, 10 pt stroke) arrived in a new Fusion design with every layer within 0.003 mm of Illustrator's bounds and offsets. The real `Send SVG to Fusion.jsx`, run in Illustrator from an installed-style tree with an active Send SVG owner record, resolved that owner, delegated with the Fusion target, cleared its global afterwards, and imported a 3 mm plate with an open window plus decimal-thickness `1.2 Label` text exactly. Test documents were closed unsaved, the temporary probe action was recycled, and Illustrator and Fusion were closed again.
- Deployed to the installed FlowCell on request (its pinned local root `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\OpenAI.Codex_2p2nqsd0c76g0\LocalCache\Roaming\FlowCell\local`; the desktop shortcut runs `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\FlowCell\flowcell_frontend.exe`). With the frontend closed, 15 files were copied and hash-verified into its `Programs`: the new Fusion handoff files, the `Send SVG to Fusion` and `Import Illustrator SVG` packages, the export JSX, both test updates, the Illustrator README, and, last, the Illustrator manifest. Blender fill and text gradient curves (2026-09-27)

- The curve box maps position along the gradient to progress through the stops: the default bottom-left to top-right line is linear. Points (2-32) are Smooth (auto-clamped), Corner, Bezier (aligned handles) or Broken (free handles); click adds, drag moves points/handles, double-click/Delete removes, Shift snaps, Alt breaks. Presets: Linear, Ease In/Out/In-Out, Hold Middle, Peak, Valley, Wave, Steps; tools: Flip, Mirror, Reset, exact Position/Value. Angle 0 runs top to bottom, 90 left to right.

Smart Axis Live saved skin (2026-10-21)
--------------------------------------
- The placement-owned Skull Live Drag skin uses the user's replacement skull image traced into a flat white inline SVG, preserving its round eye sockets, notched nose and tooth-lined grin without the red image background. The red neon fill/edge and pill clip remain. Its skeleton arm is one outlined SVG: X-crossed radius and ulna with knobbed wrist ends and shaded shafts, five carpals, and three jointed fingers plus a thumb that hooks the lower rim of the near (viewer-right) eye. Dark outlines use the surface shade, so bones stay legible over the white skull and the red Live fill.
- The arm enters straight from the right, carries the skull, then withdraws to the right at one constant horizontal speed throughout: 400cqh per second (160 CSS px/s at the saved 120x40 size). Each phase duration is its actual distance divided by that same speed; there is no rotation, easing, overshoot or settling. The skull waits until contact, then moves with the hand. At 120x40 a complete transition takes 1.18s; the long and short reaches take different times because their distances differ. Distinct reach/withdraw names in each direction preserve repeated-click playback.
- The placement visual-state map routes Press, Held and Release to one transition visual per logical state: Off uses Pressed (drag right) and On uses Release (drag left). The drag starts on pointer-down, and later triggers keep identical flags, so the latch does not restart it. The cycle, resultMatches, labels and geometry are unchanged.
- This is a saved-skin, installed Button-state and reusable skin-file update only; no frontend, backend or Blender action source changed. The October replacement changes only the skull SVG; the arm artwork, timing and placement cycle/map remain unchanged. Skin validation and browser velocity checks pass in both directions across four sizes with zero vertical movement. The inherited pill radius is retained around the animation carrier. The Blender action is not clicked during visual validation; no release rebuild or Blender reload is needed. An action response slower than the visual can still briefly restore the prior resting state until the response arrives.
- Final Skull (2026-10-31) is a reusable skin file in the installed and repo Skins folders, assigned on 2026-11-01 to the Smart Axis Live placement in the installed Button state (revision 1382; Testing Live stays in the state, unassigned). It keeps the Skull Live Drag skull, arm, timing and drag sections but replaces the pill with the sharp black box and corner lines of the user's pasted neon button. Since 2026-11-04 the saved files (backup `button-system/backups/final-skull-outline-20261104T060233Z`) behave like this: Hover traces only the red outline over 500ms and hover-off retracts it; locking drags the skull right and the red fill and glow open from the center over 500ms the moment it lands; unlocking collapses the fill and retracts the outline to its corners at once, together, while the arm drags the skull back (2026-11-04 correction, on the Live placement since 2026-11-07, revision 1449; backups `button-system/backups/final-skull-unlock-retract-20261104T063258Z` and `final-skull-unlock-retract-live-20261108T052104Z`). The Live placement got this version on 2026-11-04 (installed Button state revision 1401, after the Buttons Editor was closed; state backup `button-system/backups/final-skull-v2-live-20261104T060446Z`). The lines paint under the skull and arm so their glow cannot tint them pink at 120x40; the skull stays white on red.
- Hover and Pressed run one shared infinite `--anim-line-arm` (Release no longer does; it declares the outline at rest) (`fc-final-skull-outline-arm 1s linear 0.06s infinite`) on a wrapper around the four corner lines. The 60ms delay starts the real child CSS transitions after the visual-latch probe has settled. A plain finite Hover transition holds the presentation, and a quick click inside that window is superseded before Pressed is ever shown, so the skull teleports instead of dragging. Because the arm keeps the same name and timing in all three sections, the running animation carries across Release to Hover instead of restarting, so the outline never dips when unlocking while hovered. Pressed starts with an empty fill and its `state-snap` step opens it when the skull arrives; Release declares the fill empty so it collapses immediately; Held keeps outline and fill full.
- An authored Hover section outranks a cycle state's resting visual, so a latched placement needs `hover: held` on its On state in `visualStateMap`; without it On plus hover shows the skull back on the left. No editor control writes that map, so the Live placement's route was added by an out-of-app state edit (backup `button-system/backups/final-skull-live-20261101T051909Z`). Known limit: leaving, returning and clicking inside the 0.5s hover-off (1s in the 2026-11-01 version) still toggles but skips the drag, because the finite hover-off transitions hold the presentation. Skin validation and real-renderer flow tests pass at 200x100 and 120x40 for both versions. In the installed app the real pop-out rendered the skin, a real pointer hover traced, filled and reversed, and with On set through the activation bus hover stayed Held with the skull on the right; the Blender action was not clicked, so the real press drag is covered by the renderer tests only. No frontend, backend or Blender source changed, so no rebuild or reload is needed.
- The replacement was saved directly to the installed runtime document at revision 1258, with a full backup and unchanged unrelated state; installed and portable skin files match. Full document validation passes. Existing windows display the replacement after the user chooses Main -> Refresh. No desktop automation or app restart was performed during this direct apply; live appearance verification is left to the user.

Toolbox Pop exposes all 37 symbols including Pan; the existing main preview capacity shows only the first 36. No drawing operations were performed on the user's documents.

shortcycle (2026-09-19)
----------------------
- Windows / Utility includes shortcycle by default. Add Button can import its package manifest or entry script into any program's panel; each installed copy owns its own preserved runtime/shortcycle.json.
- First use opens a native setup dialog with one keyboard shortcut per line and Save/Cancel. Save sends no keys; subsequent presses send one shortcut and wrap through the ordered list. The cursor resets each compiled FlowCell process session while the list persists. Hold Shift while pressing to edit/reset the list. A missing target or failed input injection does not advance the cursor.
- Explicit portable single-script packages use program '*' and execution.runner 'windows-script', restricted to a package-owned .ps1 entry. Ownership remains with the destination program/panel, while installation, recovery, deletion and execution bypass that application's bridge. Updates cannot change between portable and application execution. The Windows runner supplies a per-process session token and a validated last-external-window target, including when no Pop/Fan is open.
- Validation: 107 program-source Rust tests and 13 window tests passed. Windows PowerShell 5.1 package tests cover parsing, invalid input, wrap order, reset and owner isolation; native setup tests cover invalid entries, Save and Cancel without key delivery. A disposable native window received the expected unmodified, Ctrl and Shift shortcuts, wraparound, a new-session reset and repeated single-shortcut dispatch; a missing target preserved the saved cursor. Individual creative applications and elevated targets have not been exercised.
- Deployment: compiled release rebuilt successfully; normal startup installed the enabled Windows Utility contribution. The live Main panel shows shortcycle, the installed package hashes match the catalog, and running the actual Button launched its setup helper. Verification left its shortcut list unconfigured. Existing Windows registrations need both enablement and installIfMissing for initial installation; new registrations also select the installOnAdd default.

When you are done making changes and they require a rebuild, you can stop the current running process and rebuild it as soon as you're done.

Purpose
-------
FlowCell is a Windows desktop command surface for program-specific script Buttons, editable skins and geometry, popouts/fans, program bridges, layouts, bindings, and local automation.

Current Architecture
--------------------
- Repository skill `.agents/skills/git-safe-sync/SKILL.md` documents preserving local work with a backup branch and safety commit, then fetching and merging remote changes without destructive recovery or force pushes.
- Blender action waits pause their timeout while that Blender process has a visible native dialog, allowing first-run Orca settings to be answered; actual response timeouts no longer report that Blender is closed.
- Blender Theme Absorb returns only colors available in the current Blender version; unsupported RNA fields leave staged buckets unchanged instead of failing the page response schema. Theme package version 3.0.12.
- Windows organizer project preparation accepts Microsoft's cloud-storage directory tags, including leftover OneDrive markers, while continuing to reject junctions, symbolic links, and unknown reparse tags. Setup Organization contribution version 1.3.3.
- Core dialogs and installed pages fit their complete native frame inside the monitor work area before showing, including high-DPI displays and restored positions. Minimum sizes are capped to the available client area.
- Runtime data and Programs roots use canonical filesystem identities at startup, keeping owned-source quarantine comparisons consistent through Windows AppData redirection.
- Illustrator declares Align and Rotate as optional Add Program contributions with a dedicated Toolsets destination, so Add everything installs them without relying on the currently selected panel.
- Illustrator SVG export groups a single selected path before applying the live Pathfinder effect, avoiding Illustrator's blocking ungrouped-artwork warning.
- SVG handoff completion uses extended Windows paths for atomic status-file writes, including temporary names exceeding the Windows PowerShell 260-character limit.
- Generated Windows organizer Buttons honor the installed FLOWCELL_LOCAL_ROOT, and organizer staging computes SHA-256 through .NET without depending on PowerShell module discovery.
- Blender add-on deployment explicitly creates the empty ManagedActions runtime directory required by first-install bridge discovery; Git-tracked program ZIPs cannot carry empty directories. Illustrator's standalone action helper defaults to the current v2 bridge pipe and matching start mutex.
- Background Blender jobs do not publish the shared GUI bridge status, so importing a live-tool module in a batch job cannot replace the running Blender window's process identity.
- Blender declares every catalog toolset as an optional Add Program contribution to its existing toolset panel, so Add everything includes the complete tested toolset collection on fresh installs.
- The development release launcher preserves its executable-path lookup as an array, including release-only installations, so strict PowerShell process discovery can safely distinguish this build from another installed copy.
- Illustrator bridge containment compares final filesystem paths for both Local Scripts and the requested file, matching native Rust path resolution through redirected Windows AppData and continuing to reject junction escapes.
- Installer releases use Tauri NSIS per-user setup and bundle the tracked backend, AutoHotkey runtime, helpers, config/assets and license/source notices. Installed startup resolves resources beside the executable, starts preflight and the headless backend, and keeps all writable state in `%APPDATA%\FlowCell\local`; `FLOWCELL_LOCAL_ROOT` is a direct override. Development and existing portable defaults remain unchanged.
- Installed program payloads extract into `<local root>\Programs`; an empty Windows placeholder is available on fresh launch but is not registered. Six tracked packages (including Fusion 360) ship optional ordinary Update Git Scripts contributions in Files. Runner-compatible owned entry points invoke the packaged catalog-only downloader; local conflicts/unknown files survive, unchanged upstream deletions are recycled, and staged blob-verified downloads have rollback journals. The native source picker reads the current catalog without an app restart.
- `release-tools/add-catalog-updaters.mjs` authors and validates contributions by manifest/runner; installer packaging preserves tracked-file eligibility, generates exact catalog baselines, rejects missing updater dependencies, and validates every ZIP. `windows-installer-release.yml` builds installer-only assets and runs disposable Windows install/launch/upgrade/uninstall checks. See `docs/installer-release.md`.
- Setup Organization regression fixtures canonicalize the Windows temporary directory so 8.3 aliases on CI are compared to the same physical paths returned by PowerShell.
- Frontend startup preserves the Programs root already resolved by launcher preflight; inherited development local-state paths must not redirect existing programs and panels into an empty local Programs folder.
- The bundled default Button skin is the exact supplied DefaultGray skin (light gray surface, dark text, original inset/press effects). Existing saved custom skin assignments remain owned by their canonical Button document.
- The canonical Button system is the only Button runtime. It owns main-panel Buttons, tool-set children, popouts, fans, skins, geometry, editing, activation animations, and execution targets.
- `flowcellbackend/local/button-system/button-state.json` is canonical Button state.
- Program manifests and owned source packages replace program-specific script/button inventories in application core.
- Legacy program-script records and source comment directives are migration input only; `FlowCellFrontend/src-tauri/src/program_sources/migrate.rs` is their sole reader and preserves raw legacy Button metadata through an owned migration package.
- Keep this file structural and current. Detailed behavior belongs in the focused docs and implementation.

Repository Map
--------------
- `FlowCellFrontend/src/button/` owns the Button schema, host, renderer, surfaces, editor, skins, state, runtime dispatch, geometry, popouts, fans, activation animations, and Button windows; `FlowCellFrontend/src/assets/button-animations/` owns bundled transparent sprite assets.
- `FlowCellFrontend/src/pages/main/MainButtonHost.tsx` integrates canonical Buttons into the main window.
- `FlowCellFrontend/src-tauri/src/button_state.rs` owns revisioned, atomic Button-state commits and uninstall coordination.
- `FlowCellFrontend/src-tauri/src/program_sources/` owns program manifest loading, versioned bundled-source synchronization, source install/update, active records, execution, deletion, durable transactions, and one-time migration. Bundled-source adoption can upgrade migrated records whose owned package/source paths still use the old exact absolute spelling; the transaction accepts only the absolute paths that resolve to that owner's expected Local Scripts package and rewrites the committed record into the current relative form.
- `FlowCellFrontend/src-tauri/src/main.rs` is composition only. Focused owners under `FlowCellFrontend/src-tauri/src/commands/` handle managed program/panel setup, bindings and Tool Set Button hotkeys, macros, generic file/recycling services, execution bridges, windows/topmost, and layouts. Core owns no slicer code: `commands/slicers.rs`, the `load_slicer_executable`/`launch_slicer` commands, and the `programRails.ts` slicer-launch branch were removed. The Orca, Cura, and generic Slicer Blender packages each resolve their own executable (saved `runtime/<slicer>_launcher.json`, then a well-known install-path probe, then a native picker) and spawn it detached with the exported STL paths as arguments. Dedicated Orca and generic-Slicer-as-Orca first setup uses a native Yes/No/Cancel prompt for `Allow only one OrcaSlicer instance`, states the consequence of turning it off, reads and writes Orca's real checksummed `app.single_instance` preference through a backup plus atomic replacement, and refuses to edit the preference while Orca is running. No `requires_flowcell_*_launch` response contract remains.
- Setup Organization is a folder-tree editor. Its page has two panes: `Folders` is an Explorer-style tree the user adds to, renames, deletes, and can fill from any real folder via `Load tree from folder…` (read-only against the source, breadth-first, with an optional folder cap defaulting to 12); `Files` owns the selected folder's assignments — file-group checkboxes with per-group `edit` (add/remove that group's file types under its stable ID, so every profile referencing it follows) and inline creation, a direct extension field, a `Collect all other files here` catch-all toggle, an `Ignore this folder` toggle, and `Apply`, which commits the selection so returning to that folder shows it again. A folder row displays assigned groups by name, so a 39-extension group reads as `3D` rather than its extension list. Profiles are `flowcell.windows.setup-organization.profile.v3`: an ordered flat folder list where order is routing order, each entry carrying `groupIds`, `fileTypes`, `ignored`, and `catchAll`. At most one folder may be the catch-all and it may not also be ignored; both rules fail closed at save. Shared groups split into plain file groups (`Images`, `3D` - neutral/interchange formats only) and program file groups (`Blender`, `Illustrator`, `Fusion 360`), with no extension in two groups. A profile also carries `programFolders`: each entry binds a program file group to a root-level folder named after the group, created only when at least one matching file is present anywhere in the scan, and claiming that group file types ahead of every ordinary folder route. An entry may name another saved profile to build inside that folder; when that nested profile assigns the same program group to one of its own folders, that folder becomes the destination and overrides the program folder root. A profile cannot nest itself, cannot reference a non-program group, and cannot be deleted while another profile nests it. Ignored folders are still created - ignoring governs whether files move out, not whether the folder exists - so the shipped `Program Tree` profile (`02 snapshots`, `03 archive`, `04 trash`, all ignored) builds that structure inside any program folder it is attached to. Roles, program folders, snapshot/live versioning, root bindings, resolution modes, and runtime ambiguity prompts are gone; a profile that assigns one extension to two folders is rejected at save. Profile names are unique because the name becomes the installed Button's label, and deleting a profile still referenced by an installed Button fails closed.
- Installing a profile stages and installs an ordinary Windows Button labelled with the profile name and bound to that profile ID, so it works on first press with no prompt. Pressing it resolves a target folder from its argument or the clipboard, validates any existing project marker before mutation, creates missing active profile folders, scans recursively, moves each file to the first folder in profile order claiming its extension, never overwrites, leaves unmatched files and current ignored subtrees untouched, performs enabled Recycle Bin cleanup, writes an undo journal, and atomically rewrites the project marker.
- `.flowcell-project.json` is the cross-program project marker defined by `Programs/Windows/Windows Git Scripts/Files/Setup Organization/formats/project-marker.v1.schema.json`. A Setup Organization organizer Button fully validates and reads it before every run, then rewrites it with the current profile identity, lowercase-extension-to-relative-folder destination map, and logical ignored paths, including configured program/nested-profile paths even when that program route is inactive. When enabled, the previous marker's ignored paths are the only whole-tree cleanup candidates: a path is recycled without routing its contents only when the current profile no longer owns it. Missing markers produce no stale candidates, while invalid/unsafe markers, unsafe existing stale paths, and unresolved current program/nested-profile ownership fail closed before mutation. A failed stale-tree recycle leaves the previous marker in place so the next run retries it. The Blender bridge resolves the open `.blend`'s project root and its STL/PNG export folders from that marker so program Buttons follow whichever profile organized the project; the previous fixed `01 src/00 assets` layout remains only as the fallback for projects with no marker.
- `FlowCellFrontend/src/pages/installed-page/` and `FlowCellFrontend/src-tauri/src/program_sources/installed_page.rs` own the generic installed-page sandbox, message broker, schema/capability validation, owner state, and generated-Button authorization. Product page UI and actions live only in their installed program-owned packages.
- `flowcellbackend/` is the canonical AutoHotkey/PowerShell backend and launcher root. Macro Lab resolves its tracked recorder from `flowcellbackend/helpers/RecordMacro.ahk` and launches it with the backend AutoHotkey v2 runtime.
- `flowcellbackend/local/` is ignored durable runtime state: bindings, Button state, layouts, logs, owner runtime state, program data, command payloads, and private configuration.
- `Programs/` contains manifest-defined program packages, tracked Git Scripts catalogs, ignored owned Local Scripts installs, active panel source records, and runner adapters.
- `docs/buttons.md` is the Button owner/file map. `docs/repository-layout.md`, `docs/flowcell-program-registry.md`, `docs/flowcell-toolset-manifest.md`, and `docs/blender-scripts.md` define source/package contracts. `docs/File organizer.md` is the user-facing Setup Organization how-to (open, set up, use), illustrated by `docs/file-organizer-setup.png`; package internals live in `Programs/Windows/README.md` and the package source.

Canonical Contracts
-------------------
- `button-state.json` owns Button records, placements, surfaces, skins, popouts, fans, per-Button activation animation assignments and physical bounds, and settings. No parallel Button state file is authoritative.
- Canonical Button-state reads are recovered, read-only snapshots. Main subscribes before its initial pure load, accepts that validated canonical document before attempting legacy migration or registered bundled-source synchronization, and gates its Button controls until a canonical document has been accepted; a later synchronization failure retains the accepted document and every authored skin while exposing the exact failure in a visible warning. Main publishes only a changed authoritative bootstrap revision, accepts documents monotonically by revision, and refreshes the active panel's source list when its canonical source inventory changes, after the first accepted document, and after bootstrap completes so an earlier filesystem read cannot strand newly synchronized sources off-screen. Secondary windows also subscribe before their initial pure load and never synchronize program sources as a render-time side effect.
- Button-owned Local Scripts packages are the installed source of truth. Each source owner has `Programs/<Program>/<Program> Local Scripts/<ownerButtonId>/` plus `Programs/<Program>/Panels/<Panel>/<ownerButtonId>.flowcell-source.json`.
- `<Program> Git Scripts` is a tracked catalog only. Authorized source-install flows copy into the owned Local Scripts package; ordinary catalog edits do not modify installed Buttons. A program manifest may explicitly version and synchronize named `bundledSources`, preserving existing owner IDs and presentation.
- Illustrator Utility's `Lithophane` Button sends exactly one selected linked or embedded PNG to the installation's active Blender Utility `blender.lithophane` owner without changing the Illustrator source document or selection. The nearest owning Illustrator layer or sublayer supplies the preferred Blender object name; when that name is occupied, Blender appends the first available unpadded positive integer (`New Dust`, `New Dust1`, `New Dust2`) and leaves every existing object untouched, so temporary or linked PNG filenames do not rename the result. Only a clipping path belonging to the selected PNG's actual clipped ancestor can supply its clipping-aware geometric X/Y bounds, which are passed explicitly in millimeters and take precedence over the existing `__fcsize_<width>x<height>mm` filename or pixel/DPI fallbacks. Embedded, unavailable, clipped, rotated, sheared, or mirrored rasters are captured from a temporary document with transparency preserved so Blender receives the selected appearance. Bridge readiness accepts a still-running Blender PID from the bounded status history so a later cleanup write from an overlapping old process cannot hide the ready window. Blender retains the established Lithophane Solidify thickness and default Displace strength unchanged while removing faces below the PNG alpha cutoff before solidifying a transparent silhouette, so transparent background does not become a thin rectangular sheet; fully opaque images retain the established rectangular path. Fully transparent input fails before mesh expansion, and failed creation removes its new object and unused data blocks. Both successful paths apply every generated modifier and re-fit and verify the finished physical X/Y dimensions with no live modifiers left.
- Saving a staged Tool Set from its Pop-out reconciles every new placement with its actual owning surface before validation, so one `Save Pop-out Settings` commit preserves both the launcher on its panel and the package-owned child surface. After a settings save, editor history entries hydrate missing records from the accepted canonical snapshot before settings are reapplied, so a just-installed Tool Set owner cannot be mistaken for an attempt to recreate an action or source package. Skin assignment still waits for that initial commit and identifies whether `Save Main Page Settings` or `Save Pop-out Settings` is required.
- Source installation, update, and deletion use canonical package transactions. Main's existing Add Button flow opens the Button Editor locked to the chosen Program and Panel and automatically prompts once for source content; the Editor exposes no separate import, update, or delete controls. A raw file is a script; a selected package folder must contain exactly one root `flowcell.script.json` or `flowcell.toolset.json`, and selecting a manifest package's declared entry file promotes the whole package. Companion-file, dual-manifest, and manifest-free-folder imports fail closed. Updates preserve owner/placement IDs, presentation, dependent popout/fan references, and the owner's `runtime/` state. Ordinary script/Page conversion plus installed Page ID or owner-state-format changes require delete/re-add. Tool-set slot removal/rename likewise requires delete/re-add; an explicit append-only update policy may add deterministic missing children without replacing the surviving graph, and the closed layout schema rejects malformed/unknown fields including `layout.presentation` before installation. Generated single-script updates retain the prior package until canonical Button state carries the transaction's internal source revision; exact recovery then finalizes or rolls back, and update/delete is blocked while that owner is unresolved. Delete removes the complete owned Button graph, bindings, program runtime artifacts, active record, Local package, runtime state, and managed Pop, Fan, and page windows; window cleanup waits for in-flight opens and remains an idempotent post-commit effect. Durable pending-canonical journals reconcile interrupted installs and updates against exact canonical/source identity on startup.
- The polished Buttons Editor has three panes: navigation and placement controls on the left, the complete Button workspace in the center, and the Skin Editor on the right. Every Editor open resolves to a true maximized window on the monitor selected by its initial or saved placement. Its `2093 x 1322` logical restore baseline and any supplied layout bounds are confined to that monitor's work area, and physical Button-window positions and sizes are rounded to whole pixels before Tauri window commands, so odd work-area dimensions and fractional DPI scaling cannot send invalid floating-point physical coordinates. The custom titlebar cannot drag a maximized Editor into restore state, so the window cannot spill across monitor boundaries. Program, Panel, Button, and Placement selectors keep every sibling Button visible on the selected surface. The Button selector omits Tool Set children and lists only their owner; selecting that owner exposes one `Main Page` and one `Pop-out` Placement choice. The Placement selector never exposes a separate `Fan` entry and collapses repeated concrete Pop-out occurrences to one semantic `Pop-out`, preferring the surface already being edited while preserving every underlying placement. Every canonical regular or Tool Set Pop-out exposes the same `Fan` checkbox immediately above `Save Pop-out Settings`: Pop mode shows the content with its retained owner hidden, while Fan mode creates or reveals the exact owner beside the content, focuses it for Button Label and Button Tooltip editing, and selects the existing hover-open/click-pin behavior. Changing the checkbox does not create a legacy Fan setup or change package-owned child membership. Legacy standalone Fan surfaces remain supported by their runtime and settings paths but are omitted from the Placement selector. The Editor exposes no source/action library or legacy Fan-surface construction/membership controls and never fabricates a legacy Fan surface. Tool Set Pop-outs remain classified as Pop-out settings and use `Save Pop-out Settings`; `Save Main Page Settings`, legacy `Save Fan Settings`, or `Save Pop-out Settings` opens a native file-save dialog and writes strict `flowcell-button-settings/v1` content under the exact ordinary `.json` filename chosen by the user in `flowcellbackend/local/Button editor/<Program>/<Panel>/Main`, `Fan`, or `Pop`. Save and Load show that leaf filename rather than expanding it to a FlowCell-specific suffix or displaying its full path. Program and Panel path components are validated. A one-time local migration moves validated flat saves into this hierarchy, while remembered legacy Pop paths resolve to migrated files without changing their choice IDs. The file captures the selected surface's exact ordered Button membership and complete presentation: frame and uniform size; placement geometry, sizing, text, highlights, cycles, and visual maps; each effective literal skin; shared Button label, activation behavior, and activation animation; the Pop-out's Pop/Fan interaction mode and exact optional owner placement; and applicable container behavior. It excludes execution targets, action/source packages, live activation state, and application-managed desktop window layout. The same action performs the revision-safe scoped canonical commit, preserving concurrent external deletion and action identity; it is not the application-wide Save Layout operation. Every skin, assignment, Button Text, animation, and default write also keeps its requested scope frozen and retries against a freshly loaded canonical revision when another window commits first, so a normal `Button state changed before Save` race does not discard the Editor action or overwrite unrelated Pop/Fan changes. The existing Edit/Run switch remains in the left rail so the workspace can still execute Buttons and preview their activation behavior.
- A source-backed single-script Button with no concrete Pop-out still exposes one semantic `Pop-out` Placement choice. Choosing it materializes the existing regular-Pop starter layout as an undoable working draft and selects its new placement; merely viewing the selector changes nothing, and `Save Pop-out Settings` remains the explicit file and canonical commit boundary.
- The Editor's Program selector also exposes the synthetic `FlowCell Main Page` scope without passing it through registered-program panel lookup or source import. Its Panel selector divides non-deployable application controls into `Program Rail`, `Panel Rail`, `Button Section Rail`, and `Header Buttons`: each registered program has its own stable canonical Program Rail presentation; Panel Rail aliases the real per-program panel-owner Buttons individually; Button Section Rail excludes installed script Buttons; and Header Buttons includes Minimize, Maximize, and Close. Ordinary registered-program discovery may add or reconcile known controls but never deletes an absent program's presentation; editing a Program Button label changes only that label, while program removal removes the matching Main Page control in the same canonical transaction. The Main Page remains authoritative for dynamic rail/header slots, live count labels, disabled state, and group-aware contained layout; each selected canonical control independently owns its skin, text, animation, authored size, and position, and the live Main Page consumes its saved placement coordinates and individual height without imposing one shared header height.
- Main's `Theme` Button is the only Universal Theme Editor entry point. Its Target selector contains `FlowCell` plus every currently discovered installed program, so Blender is an ordinary dropdown choice rather than a separate Theme-page integration. FlowCell targets use the extensible page registry (`Main`, `MacroLab`, and `Binds` initially); `Main` includes its fixed rails/owners and every program-panel Button surface rendered in the Main content area, while excluding Pop/Fan surfaces. Installed-program targets use `All Panels and Pop-outs`, `Pop-outs Only`, or one discovered panel. All Panels and a selected panel retain Main panel-owner, panel, regular Pop-out, Fan, and Tool Set Pop-out occurrences in their respective scope; Pop-outs Only spans every panel in the selected program but includes only regular and Tool Set Pop-out surfaces, excluding Main/panel surfaces, legacy standalone Fans, and every other program. The editor is one bulk appearance surface: it reports the exact scoped occurrences, exposes fixed universal Surface and Text channels, and applies one deterministic color/gradient/scatter rule to every matched Button in the complete scope without a skin-compatibility prerequisite. Profiled and recognized legacy Surface/Text variables receive exact host overrides; an unprofiled Surface receives a transient render-only paint map that moves its non-text paint around the selected hue while retaining geometry, relative shading, source alpha, shadows, and animation, and selected alpha multiplies fallback paint opacity. Unprofiled Text changes only the injected label. Neither path edits or reassigns the saved skin. Surface is the initial channel. The editor exposes readable swatch-plus-opacity controls and makes the whole page scrollable. Channel, spread, and scatter inputs snapshot their primitive DOM values before React functional state updates, so changing a color channel or range input cannot outlive the event and crash the Theme window. The Theme Editor exposes no Hover/Active enablement or highlight-color controls; existing theme files containing those fields remain readable and applicable for compatibility. The saved-skin selector lists only saved `.flowcell-button-skin.txt` files and changes only which skin Button occurrences use: unchecked affects only the selected occurrence, while `Apply this saved skin to all Buttons in the current scope` assigns it to every in-scope Button regardless of each Button's current skin. FlowCell `Main` means every Main-page Button; a program's All Panels or selected-panel scope likewise means every Button in that exact scope. Buttons outside the scope remain unchanged. Assignment clears older placement-owned Surface, Text, hover-color, and active-color overrides so the newly assigned skin begins with its authored colors, while preserving explicit hover/active On/Off truth. It never edits a saved skin definition or changes Button actions, text, geometry, activation behavior, or managed-window layout. Strict version 2 `.flowtheme.json` files store registered page tokens/assets, explicit placement theme overrides, deterministic gradient/scatter participation, and only requested saved-skin assignments; version 1 themes normalize on load. Open Theme and Save Theme start in the saved-theme folder, `layouts/Main Page` under the FlowCell local root, which `get_flowcell_theme_directory` resolves (creating it when missing). No-op Apply creates no cloned skins. Theme state and custom authorized background images remain separate from strict managed-window Layout snapshots. The Main Page card's Preset menu loads the built-in Concrete theme (concrete texture plus a visible fill/border on every rail) into the draft without touching Buttons or existing themes, and Background Image offers Default, Concrete (bundled texture), None or Custom image.
- `Load Main Page Settings`, legacy `Load Fan Settings`, or `Load Pop-out Settings` sits directly below its matching Save action and opens the selected Program/Panel's corresponding `Main`, `Fan`, or `Pop` folder. A load must match the placement category and concrete surface subtype, every saved Button ID must still be canonically installed, and cross-surface reuse must also match the selected Program and Panel; a file tied to the same stable surface ID remains valid across Program/Panel renames. It replaces only the selected surface with the file's exact Button amount, order, frame, presentation, and container behavior as one undoable Editor draft; Buttons not listed are removed from that surface, saved Buttons are restored without importing or changing their execution targets, and `Save … Settings` remains the explicit canonical commit. Tool Set child membership stays package-owned, so its settings cannot add or remove actions. A Tool Set settings file may also serve as a presentation template for another installed Tool Set with the same child count: children map by declared slot and then saved order, while the target owner, actions, fields, labels, ownership, and activation behavior remain intact. Directly below Load are `Load … Default` and `Update … Default`. Each concrete surface keeps its existing internal default under the legacy flat type folder: the first visit lazily captures whatever that surface currently has in committed Button state, Load stages that snapshot, and Update atomically replaces it from the current Editor draft after a revision check.
- Main's `Save Layout` and `Load Layout` native pickers own `flowcellbackend/local/layouts/Main Page` as their default folder and use the Main-specific machine-local key `flowcell.main-page-layout-directory.v1` when remembering a deliberately chosen directory. The Main-page buttons are only the trigger: a strict current layout stores the exact open managed secondary-window set—Button Editor, Pop-out, Fan, and installed Page—with Pop/Fan semantic physical bounds and Editor/Page native physical bounds. Before closing anything, Load validates every bound and re-resolves every saved Page through its active installed descriptor; it then closes the current managed set, awaits every managed window's actual destruction before a stable label can be reused, and reopens the complete saved set through the existing window helpers. Stateful Button listener teardown observes Tauri's asynchronous unlisten result, retries once across a listener-table race, and contains final teardown failure so a closing host cannot trigger the global fatal screen. A restored fixed Pop/Fan canvas installs its move and DPI listeners before taking the authoritative startup-position snapshot, so the native host's hidden move from its temporary creation origin to the monitor canvas cannot strand the visible Button surface at that temporary origin. Editor/Page move and resize events retain their last usable native bounds so a minimized window is not omitted, while minimized-sentinel coordinates are rejected in layout files. Layout files never store or mutate Main-window bounds, Main navigation, Main Button placement, canonical Button/action state, or transient settings-preview windows. The picker folder remains separate from the Button Editor's Program/Panel-scoped Main, Fan, and Pop settings-file folders.
- `Animation` opens a dedicated page without reducing the existing activation-animation feature. Its selector includes `No animation` and every registered preset, and Apply either clears or assigns the selected animation through an animation-scoped canonical save that leaves unsaved placement geometry untouched. A top-right X exits the Animation page and closes any open setup presenter without clearing the assignment. The page retains the complete position-and-size setup flow, saved physical bounds, explicit setup close action, and runtime playback. `Plus Rise` setup renders only the static PNG at its true maximum animation size; app-managed invisible edge/corner resizing remains locked to the source's 283:295 ratio, and the shared Button record saves that semantic maximum-size rectangle in whole physical desktop pixels. Playback derives a separate larger cursor-ignored transparent travel canvas, then uses 61 linearly timed motion samples over 1.2 seconds to rise continuously from faint/small through opaque/full size before shrinking and fading out. Runtime requests from Main, Pop, Fan, and Editor use deterministic live-window coordinator election and are generation-scoped. Only the primary activation event triggers it; selection and paired release/hover events do not. `Star Wars Lightsaber` adds the original bundled 480 x 268 GIF as a separate selectable animation, with ratio-locked position/size setup and one 3.01-second cycle per playback. Each playback reloads its GIF resource so it starts from the beginning; settings files and popup-window routing accept both registered presets. The Animation page offers Every press, Every N presses, and Random interval with editable whole press counts from 1 to 10000. Apply saves the frequency per Button; its elected playback coordinator persists the pending interval and progress in shared WebView local storage across sessions, and a changed frequency starts a fresh count on its next activation. Random intervals include both bounds and reroll after playback. Older assignments retain their defaults: Star Wars uses random 5-50 presses and Plus Rise uses every press. Animation has a 0-100% Transparency slider; Apply saves per-Button opacity with the assignment, and both position/size preview and playback use it. Older assignments and settings files retain 0% overall transparency until changed. The same Animation page includes a draggable fade curve (playback time versus opacity), numeric point controls, point insertion/removal, Reset fade, and No fade. Missing curves default to a smooth fade in over the first 20% and fade out over the last 20% of each playback; explicit curves save with the Button and settings files. The curve multiplies overall opacity on the playback stage while preserving the preset sprite motion and GIF cycle; position/size setup remains visible at the chosen overall opacity. Fade animation cancellation follows request replacement, edit mode, and unmount. Activation requests are dispatched before awaiting the Button action result.
- Every real program panel has one source-free `panel-owner` Button on that program's main panel-rail surface. It is the actual main-page panel selector, including Utility, and panel/program folder lifecycle creates, renames, or removes it; generic Button deletion cannot remove it. Panel discovery during ordinary program navigation is read-only: it publishes the filesystem panel list immediately, uses a fallback panel control while canonical presentation loads, and never prunes or rewrites owners from a potentially stale discovery snapshot. Add Program always offers an `Any program` path with blank Program-name and executable-path fields; one `Add program` action silently validates the entered name and exact host EXE, generates a registration-only manifest with that normalized EXE filename/stem as its sole process match rather than the full path, rejects a process name already claimed by another valid Program package, and creates no Panels or bundled Buttons. The application need not be running, while an unknown unregistered executable elsewhere with the same filename remains a residual match risk. Panels and Buttons can be added afterward through the normal actions. Its separate Managed package path retains register-only, add-everything, and exact custom plans from an available package manifest, with explicit panel/contribution selection, dependency closure, destination panels, host-EXE confirmation, and a reviewed effect list. Add Panel creates an empty panel or copies one validated external folder without modifying the source; it rejects links, traversal, and FlowCell-owned runtime artifacts, while an existing case-insensitive panel match is an idempotent reconcile.
- A panel owner's Main placement and each saved legacy Fan placement reuse that same Button identity while retaining placement-specific geometry and skin overrides. Existing saved Fans remain fully supported: Run preview uses the saved open, close, and pinned rules; the default starts on the real owner, hover opens and hover-out collapses, owner click pins, and the next pinned click collapses. Separately, each Pop-out in Fan mode owns one real placement-specific owner on the same authored surface; Edit shows it alongside the content, Pop mode hides it without deleting its geometry, and runtime collapses to that exact placement. Tool Set child/member arrays never absorb the owner.
- A fan setup's `collapsedPanelOwnerBounds` desktop anchor is optional: it is absent until the user space-drags the collapsed fan, which persists the visible semantic frame in real physical desktop pixels. The owner frame is derived from its applied visual envelope and effective WebView pixel ratio while preserving the owner's surface origin, so shadows and other overflow do not clip or shift the later expanded frame. Reusing an expanded resting frame never replaces that collapsed owner anchor, so repeated hover expansion and collapse keep the owner at the same physical point. Unanchored fans open centered over the launching window, and loading strips legacy `{left: 0, top: 0}` placeholder anchors.
- Legacy Fan Options exposes additive Fan styles. `Spin Buttons as they fan out` is unchecked by default, including when older state/settings files are normalized. When enabled, every non-owner placement spins from the fixed owner into its arbitrary saved grid position and reverses before the Fan collapses; Fan Options' Run mode previews that same lifecycle. The placement-wrapper animation works with any skin, keeps moving Buttons inert until settled, bounds the total stagger window for large grids, honors reduced-motion preferences, and does not alter Fan membership or geometry. The style is persisted independently so more Fan-style checkboxes can be added later.
- The main-page Fan button prioritizes an exact nonempty panel selection (single-script Buttons plus Tool Set owners), labels itself `Fan (N)`, creates/reuses that member-set Fan, and opens it. With no selection it retains the saved/default workflow: no saved setup creates a persisted generic Fan, one opens directly, and several show the saved-setup menu. `ensureFanSetup` is idempotent per member set.
- A Button failure inside a native Fan forwards its execution result to the Fan window's existing runtime-error surface, so long-running child actions cannot fail silently even if the Fan collapses before they finish. Main and Pop retain their own existing error presentation paths. Fixed transparent Button hosts never use synchronous browser alerts for execution failures because those dialogs fall outside the selective native hit-test inventory and can deadlock the entire surface.
- Main's Program context-menu rename is presentation-only: it edits that Program Button label and leaves the real folder, manifest, registration, source identity, Panels, bindings, windows, and runner data untouched. Panel folder rename preserves stable Button and presentation IDs while migrating its canonical/native scope. The retained backend program-package rename transaction still uses one durable outer journal with exact previous/next bindings, registration bytes or absence, matching macro bytes, and canonical Button documents for callers that explicitly invoke that lower-level operation.
- Add Program and Add Panel use durable native journals around registration, enabled-contribution state, filesystem publication, normal source installs, and canonical owner commits; startup finalizes only an exact canonical/source match and otherwise rolls back the proved transaction. A generated registration-only Program package is created only after the prepared journal exists, carries a transaction-identity marker until commit, and never overwrites an existing case-insensitive package or shared program ID; before commit FlowCell re-proves its exact marker, manifest, complete generated tree, native rail registration, and enabled-contribution state, while rollback requires every present generated artifact and native state to match an allowed journal boundary. Drift or reparse points fail closed and preserve the package, and rollback sends it to the Recycle Bin only while that proof matches. A valid same-name package may be selected through Managed package, while a malformed blocking folder must be repaired, removed, or avoided with a distinct name. Add Program holds one source-quarantine guard across its complete selected-source batch and calls the caller-locked installer, while standalone source operations acquire that same guard themselves, preserving serialization without recursively locking the non-reentrant guard. Program removal writes a strict durable journal containing the exact pre/post bindings, exact enabled-contribution registration file state, and expected canonical owner IDs before unregistering; after canonical/source recovery, startup restores both native pre-delete states when that exact owner set remains or finishes unregistration and recycles the enabled-registration file when the exact scoped owner set is absent, while partial owners or unrelated state drift fail closed. An Add Program rollback treats a planned source that never reached its active-record publish boundary as absent, so a prepared transaction can still restore bindings and enabled state, recycle its created panel, and clear its journal. Panel deletion likewise prepares a strict journal and an identity marker before canonical cleanup, then recycles only the marked immediate-child panel after the exact owners and active records are gone; startup cancels while the exact owners remain and finalizes after they are absent. Empty expected-owner sets are accepted only for a proved-empty canonical scope and authorize immediate recovery finalization because no canonical commit bit exists. The available program package is kept, and obsolete-window closing is best-effort after commit.
- User-facing deletions go to the Recycle Bin. Source transactions use recoverable JSON writes and durable quarantine journals; startup finishes committed cleanup or rolls back pre-commit work after interruption.
- `flowcell.program.json` defines program identity, explicit managed-setup Panels, folder roles, allowed source types, runner, native owner policy, shortcut profile, and optional versioned Button contributions. Registration-only generated manifests use a `plain-` program ID, no Panels or contributions, the generic Windows runner, and one exact normalized EXE-filename/stem process name rather than the full path. Preflight rejects a process name already claimed by another valid Program package, but scoped topmost cannot distinguish an unknown unregistered executable elsewhere that shares the same filename. Add Program persists the exact enabled contribution set under `flowcellbackend/local/program-registration/<programId>.json`; synchronization considers only that registered/enabled set. Only `required` makes a contribution mandatory in Add Program, `installOnAdd` is a setup default, and `installIfMissing` is solely the repair policy for an already enabled contribution.
- `flowcell.script.json` describes a single-script catalog package and may contain a strict `page` declaration. `flowcell.toolset.json` describes one owner, child slots/payloads, optional tool-set layout/fields, and an optional package-owned `stateQuery` restricted to a declared child slot plus the read-only `status` action; page-enabled Buttons are script packages, not tool sets.
- An installed page is not another Button type or a product route. Its ordinary single-script owner receives the generic `open-installed-page` target, and every open/action re-resolves that exact active record and Button-owned package. Its managed native label includes a hash of the exact owner identity so sanitized or truncated owner names cannot collapse distinct Page windows. The page manifest declares only contained resources, strict request/response schemas, capabilities, owner-state format, optional shared program-data namespace, supported data formats, refresh events, window settings (including optional fixed `alwaysOnTop` host behavior), and package config. Missing `alwaysOnTop` defaults off; an opted-in Page bypasses program-scoped topmost registration on ordinary open and layout restore. Installed-page host styles stay class-scoped so their opaque page background cannot leak onto the global document roots used by transparent Pop/Fan windows.
- Installed page content runs in a raw WRY child WebView with no Tauri initialization scripts. The child reuses its parent Tauri WebView2 environment so opening a page does not cold-start a second browser/GPU process group or executable-local user-data folder; its controller remains InPrivate and isolated from Tauri globals. A package-only custom protocol serves sanitized entry HTML and declared resources under a deny-by-default CSP; native handlers block navigation, new windows, downloads, drag/drop, clipboard/browser features, and developer tools. Before any package script runs, a nonce-bound default-world raw-IPC proof must attest the isolated page URL, bridge globals, and absence of Tauri, Node, and privileged globals; Core fails closed until that proof passes. Closing a page first awaits raw-child unmount while its parent HWND is alive, then destroys the host and acknowledges its exact destroyed event, so WRY teardown never runs re-entrantly from parent destruction. The two-phase trusted broker validates installed identity, declared action/capability, and request/response schemas before and after any generic host capability. A page may call another installed program page only through a manifest-fixed provider handler: the caller must declare the exact capability published by the target action, the provider must be the active matching bundled page source, both request and response schemas are revalidated, and mismatched, ambiguous, or missing providers fail closed.
- Blender Theme, Save Blender, and Illustrator Layer Tree are ordinary page-enabled script packages owned by their program catalogs. Blender Theme keeps its existing package-owned page and has no Universal Theme Editor control; the Universal Theme Editor opens only from Main and discovers Blender as a normal installed-program Target option. Save Blender opts into the generic Page host's fixed always-on-top behavior and saves an already named `.blend` in place. For an untitled file, `Create new project` asks for a project name, remembered parent folder, and an optional canonical Setup Organization profile selected by human-readable name from the provider's profile list; UUID profile filenames remain internal, while Save Blender persists the stable profile ID. Entered project/file names are sanitized before use by deleting whitespace and Windows-invalid filename characters, trimming terminal periods, limiting the result to 120 characters, and prefixing reserved Windows device names. A direct new project creates `<parent>/<sanitized-name>/Blender` and saves `<sanitized-name>.blend` there; a profile-backed project asks the Windows provider to prepare the real profile tree and preserves its planned Blender route. `Add to existing project` keeps the editable Blender filename prompt and accepts any selected existing project folder: a valid marker's existing `.blend` route wins, otherwise its still-available saved profile may supply one Blender/static/catch-all destination, and an unassigned or unmarked folder gets a `Blender` subfolder before the `.blend` is saved. This path creates only the one required destination path and never reruns organization, scans or moves project files, performs cleanup, creates unrelated profile folders, or rewrites the marker. The same Blender-owned validation can construct a sanitized, non-overwriting `.blend` target from a provider-prepared existing-project destination for program-to-program handoffs. Existing-project mode and its selected folder are deliberately not persisted. Later new-project saves reuse parent/profile settings, clear the name after success, and expose `Change Settings` to reopen the full form. Theme and Layer Tree keep their existing package-owned UI, assets, actions, configuration, helpers, adapters, and lifecycle behavior; Core contains only generic installed-page infrastructure.
- Windows Setup Organization is an ordinary Windows-owned page package. Its shared namespace under `flowcellbackend/local/program-data/windows/setup-organization/` owns V3 folder-tree profiles, reusable plain/program file groups, authenticated short-lived Button stages, and undo data; per-page preferences remain in the owner runtime. The page's literal `Install Button` action saves the active profile, stages an ordinary six-field script manifest, embeds the shared profile ID only in `organize_folder.ps1`, authenticates that stage, and installs or updates one stable package-derived Windows Button through the normal source transaction.
