Its provider-only `list-profiles` action exposes sorted profile IDs and human-readable names without storage paths, while `prepare-target` accepts an empty project root, canonical saved profile, and planned extension, runs the same organizer engine without a placeholder file, writes the genuine marker/tree, and returns the selected destination directory for program-owned consumers such as Save Blender. The separate `prepare-existing-target` provider accepts any safe existing project folder and never invokes that organizer engine: it uses an existing valid marker route when present, otherwise resolves one available saved-profile route, falls back to a `Blender` subfolder for an unassigned `.blend` target, and creates only that returned destination path without scanning, moving, recycling, journaling, or rewriting project content or metadata. Installed Windows page capabilities convert ownership-checked canonical `\\?\` paths back to ordinary DOS/UNC spelling before invoking Windows PowerShell 5.1, whose provider cannot resolve verbatim paths. The generated Button resolves the first nonempty copied-folder entry and otherwise falls back to a copied text path, applies its referenced shared profile, recycles the retired `organize-folder.profile.json` and `organize-folder.undo.json` artifacts after routing, and writes `.flowcell-project.json`. Two adjacent permanent per-profile switches can recycle empty folders absent from the current profile and, dependently, ignored folders recorded by the previous marker that the current profile no longer owns. Current static, program, nested-profile, and ignored paths remain protected; stale ignored-tree contents are skipped by routing and the whole stale tree goes to the Recycle Bin, ordinary cleanup preserves folders containing unmoved files, and every production removal uses the Recycle Bin. Deleting the Button removes its owner lifecycle while leaving the profile reusable.
- Numbered `bindings.ini` records have three target contracts. Existing single-script bindings remain path-based `ScriptPath` records registered and executed by the resident AutoHotkey backend even when the Tauri/frontend surface is closed. Tool-set owner and child bindings instead store `TargetKind=tool-set-owner|tool-set-child` plus canonical `ButtonId` and `OwnerButtonId`; only the running Tauri process registers their supported keyboard accelerators and refreshes them immediately after Binds changes. An owner hotkey press invokes the canonical managed-popout toggle directly, independent of Main's selection-only owner click, while a child press targets the active expanded owner and feeds the mounted child through its normal ButtonHost lifecycle. Binds keeps top-level navigation available but exposes children only for the selected Tool Set, and Current Panel Binds shows exactly that owner/child group. Illustrator declares one bind-only `Actions / Set Anchor` package action backed by its shipped helper and loaded by the generic Core bind adapter: its shortcut is stored under `ActionHotkeys`, registered pass-through only while Illustrator is foreground so the native Illustrator command still receives the same keystroke, and completes an immediate foreground automation call before returning so the keypress selection's visible-bounds box and center cannot be replaced by a later selection during process-launch delay. All native bindings mutations and source-removal rollbacks share one serialized document lane; hotkey synchronization reads its desired snapshot inside its own serialized lane, and legacy AutoHotkey binding edits preserve non-binding INI sections such as program registration and action hotkeys. A failed native registration rolls the binding change back, and no child payload or field snapshot is stored in the binding. Binds reads canonical Button state through a tolerant projection: optional canonical strings accept an explicit `null` exactly as they accept a missing key, and popout-unit ownership is modeled as optional because only `tool-set` units carry an owner while regular units are unowned and persist `"ownerButtonId": null`. Canonical data that Binds never consults therefore cannot abort the workspace load or a Tool Set shortcut save. Script binding targets are persisted in ordinary DOS/UNC spelling: ownership resolution rewrites installed source paths to verbatim `\\?\` form, but `bindings.ini` is executed by the resident AutoHotkey backend, and Windows PowerShell 5.1 cannot provider-resolve a verbatim path — `Join-Path` fails on one, so any script deriving paths from `$PSScriptRoot` dies before running. Binds strips the prefix both where it surfaces the target and where it writes `ScriptPath`, so an already-persisted verbatim path heals on the next rewrite.
- Macro binding uses the same compact `Target / Program / Panel / Macro / Shortcut / Bind` row as Button binding. The selected Program and Panel filter the managed macro inventory, Current Panel Binds and Global Macros show each matching macro with its visible `ActionHotkeys` shortcut even when that panel has no script Buttons, and Macro Lab refreshes preserve the current ownership scope. Program and Panel identify the owned macro; macro hotkeys remain global `ActionHotkeys` at runtime rather than ProgramTab-scoped numbered bindings.
- Page owner state is stored only at `<Program> Local Scripts/<ownerButtonId>/runtime/installed-page-state.json` in a strict envelope. Deleting a Theme or Layer Tree Button removes that state, its Local Scripts package, active record, bindings, canonical graph, registered runtime artifacts, and page window through the standard Button-deletion transaction; no product-specific Core state survives.
- Normal execution resolves only a valid `.flowcell-source.json` record whose installed source is inside its matching owner Local Scripts package.

- Blender Theme's package-owned page uses the original bucket and action wording and opens directly at Theme Palette without the retired decorative header. It keeps four slightly taller buckets per row with each bucket's swatch, hex field, and Apply control on one line; Gradient 2 keeps its enabled checkbox directly beside its name with no separate description row. Darkness profile controls sit directly after Load Buckets, the full Place Picture section stays in the compact default view, and HDRI stacks below it. Every rendered action Button has a package-owned explanatory tooltip, including its specific bucket name where applicable. `Remove Grid` disables only the fake grid while keeping the picture and fake gizmos active; grid visibility persists with project/startup picture state. Theme apply writes the active theme and live Place Picture as one owner-runtime startup bundle, and package load applies or clears its picture before committing the theme, so a saved theme cannot restore with an older picture.

- Blender Theme has a separate `Popped Button Colors` section for aggregate Surface colors from action Buttons in open Blender Pop-out and Fan windows. One action row is ordered `Rescan`, `Apply Buckets`, `Refill`, `Scatter`, then `Toggle Text`; `Apply Gradient` and `Apply Text Gradient` live in the separate Button Fill and Button Text boxes, each with a curve box and Angle (Theme 3.0.20). Every persisted gradient stop is now directly visible and editable in an ordered, horizontally scrollable row from `Top`, through `Color 2` to `Color N-1`, to `Bottom`; the synchronized `Gradient Colors` range and whole-number input accepts 2 through 16 and redraws every deterministically resampled stop immediately. `Rescan` enumerates actual live windows instead of every saved definition: expanded regular and Tool Set Pop-outs contribute rendered placements, while authored Pop-out Fans and legacy standalone Fans contribute the exact Fan-surface owner and all members in both collapsed and expanded states. Closed or minimized windows, the separate Main/Panel occurrence of a Fan owner, other-program surfaces, and Button Editor preview Fans remain excluded. Collapsed renderers mirror the real owner placement's theme override onto their synthetic display placement, so the applied owner color survives Fan collapse without changing the Main occurrence. Canonical windows read and save canonical per-placement overrides, while Main settings-backed Open Pops read and repaint through their isolated live draft without rewriting the settings file. Equal effective roots are grouped into aggregate buckets (the named individual Button list was removed in 3.0.20), and tint-only skins expose read-only non-text material swatches instead of a guessed root. `Apply Buckets` is bound to an in-memory scan token plus an exact live-scope fingerprint and fails closed when the windows, draft, colors, placements, or canonical revision change. `Apply Gradient` uses all persisted ordered colors as smooth adjacent segments, retains the Spread/Scatter controls, and reuses the current seed. Its persisted `Whole Screen` checkbox (formerly Screen Top-to-Bottom) is off by default; authored Fans must be expanded while that mode is applied so their live full frame is available, while legacy Fans map their saved surface envelope from the collapsed owner anchor. `Refill` freshly samples exactly the requested Gradient Colors count from the current Theme image, fails closed when too few distinct colors are available, stores and displays the sampled order, advances the seed, and immediately applies the gradient without requiring a prior Rescan. `Scatter` redistributes the currently present bucket colors by advancing the persisted seed and hashing it with each hidden placement ID, so the mapping remains deterministic until Scatter is pressed again. `Toggle Text` derives one black-or-white result from the current live aggregate scope and writes only per-placement Text overrides, preserving Surface colors and saved skins without storing a page toggle. The section persists its gradient color count and ordered stops, seed, controls, screen-gradient checkbox, buckets, and internal mapping in owner state and never changes saved skins, actions, membership, or geometry.

- The Universal Theme Editor has separate `Highlight` and `Glow` sections with four scope-wide percentage sliders: brightness on hover, brightness when active, glow on hover, and glow when active. Every slider shows its current number, immediately previews on every Button in the exact current FlowCell Main, program, panel, or Pop-outs Only scope, and defaults to 75%. Highlight spans 0-1000%; 100% is the full-strength reference and 1000% applies ten times that lift, while Glow remains independently bounded to 0-100%. Their optional per-placement values persist through Apply, Save Theme, and Open Theme; old files retain the 75% defaults and the original combined amount remains a compatibility fallback. These controls do not change hover/active enablement or highlight colors. Assigning a saved skin preserves all four amounts and never edits the skin source. Highlight and glow are render-only and do not alter Button geometry, measurement, hit testing, actions, or animation sequencing.

Button Host And Skin Contract
-----------------------------
- The functional host owns interaction state, keyboard/pointer behavior, accessibility, validation, dispatch, and lifecycle.
- A placement may own one host-run activation cycle containing at least two ordered logical states; two states are the ordinary On/Off toggle, while any larger count is the same cycle model. State 1 is the initial session state. Each state owns one stable ID, label, `press`/`hover`/`release` advance trigger, one canonical latched resting visual supplied by the assigned skin, and optional partial-JSON `resultMatches`. Ordinary cycles advance locally and wrap to State 1. A result-mapped cycle instead waits for an authoritative action response; each newly opened expanded Tool Set resets its result-mapped placements to State 1, and later child responses set the exact index rather than guessing the next one. A declared package-owned read-only `stateQuery` is not run automatically during open/expansion. The host that receives an authoritative response applies it locally before broadcasting it to other mounted copies, so a Pop does not remain at State 1 merely because cross-window transport has no active Main coordinator. Real Hover, Pressed, Held, Play, and Release input temporarily renders its nonempty authored skin state, then settles back to the latched visual; an empty transient section falls through to the next authored input state or the configured resting visual, keeping a completed Hover target continuously applied instead of replaying its transition. A placement-owned visual-state map may key configured cycle-state IDs to route those transient triggers to different authored visuals without changing the stable cycle visual, index, label, or authoritative result matching. Once an authored Pressed, Play, or Release presentation is requested, native window preparation must commit it to the skin before a fast pointer-up or action result may supersede it; finite motion then completes from the Web Animations completion signal before the newest requested appearance is applied. Functional Error and Disabled remain the highest-priority desired visuals without interrupting already-latched finite motion, and edit-focus selection does not replace a configured visual. The current index remains session-only and keyed by placement ID across mounted windows; response mappings and query declarations do not persist the live index or alter skin source. One press/release gesture can advance at most once even when another mounted copy advances in between; cancel is not release, and a real hover entry advances at most once until leave. Main's selection-only script Buttons may advance a Hover state on real entry, keep ordinary single selection clicks inert, and synthesize one Press/Release gesture only after successful double-click execution.
- The Skin Editor's `Button States & Behavior` section exposes a state count from 2 through 64, one Advance on dropdown per state, then a State and skin-dependent Visual state dropdown with an immediate real renderer preview. A result-mapped state adds one compact `Action: value` line whose tooltip shows its complete response matches. The visual list contains Base plus canonical sections authored by the active working skin; choosing one changes only that placement cycle and never rewrites skin code. Button Text uses those exact logical states for per-state labels and previews the selected state's assigned visual immediately. Save Settings persists the cycle. Button Text's bottom `Apply All` remains text-only and requires changed cycle IDs to be saved first. It saves each shared Button Tooltip regardless of cycle configuration; with a configured cycle it saves only placement-state labels and preserves the shared base Button label, while without one it saves the base label and mirrors it into legacy activation-state labels so the compatibility runtime and preview agree. Legacy Button-owned activation behavior remains readable only as compatibility fallback for placements without a configured activation cycle.
- Main's Button section separates selection from execution: one ordinary click toggles that exact script, macro, or Tool Set owner in the current multi-selection, latching the skin's authored pressed state with no host outline unless that placement owns a cycle whose selected visual remains authoritative, while a double-click runs that Button's primary action directly on Main. Main suppresses the Button's authored hover/press/click actions and activation effect for the ordinary selection gesture, including Buttons whose `pressDown` event normally owns execution. `Fan (N)` retains selection-driven Fan creation/opening. `Open Pop`, immediately left of `Pop`, opens a native picker in the selected `<Program>/<Panel>/Pop` settings folder and opens the selected regular or Tool Set settings file as an isolated temporary draft window; it does not load into an Editor surface or mutate or commit canonical Button state. Its ordinary `button-popout` window participates in Save Layout with its selected file, choice, panel owner, and semantic bounds, so Load validates and rebuilds that draft at its saved position before replacing the current managed layout. Main remembers only `{ path, choiceId }` in machine-local storage under the stable panel-owner Button ID. `Pop` reloads and opens that panel's last-used file independent of Main selection; when none is remembered, it creates or reuses a canonical starter Pop containing that panel's installed single-script Buttons and opens it. Once popped out, an ordinary click runs the Button through its normal runtime host.
- Skins are render-only and isolated. They cannot execute actions, mutate state, own bindings, or define source targets.
- Every skin contains exactly one measurable `[data-core]`, zero or one `{{label}}` token inside it, and optionally one `data-hit-shape` descendant when the semantic core is a larger layout wrapper than the intended pointer shape. Textless and animation-only skins receive no synthesized text or fallback face; the saved Button label still supplies the accessible name.
- A skin that supplies a complete alternate appearance for a logical Button state is authored as one paste-ready composite: every alternate face stays inside one stable `[data-core]`, and state declarations or CSS custom properties reveal the intended face. A skin may never embed another functional Button, another `[data-core]`, a per-state hit target, or a runtime skin swap for an alternate state; the optional single `data-hit-shape` remains stable across every state.
- Skin authoring uses a neutral render-only core whose resting body matches the intended clickable footprint. Browser controls are forbidden inside skin markup. Newly authored packed skins may not invent transparent layout gutters, while literal conversions preserve the source control's complete visual box model, including authored padding or transparent depth space that positions its face/base/press travel; only outer demo/page spacing is removed. Inherited source typography is frozen to its resolved pixel font size/line height so natural geometry stays identical across Editor/Main/Pop/Fan. Pseudo-elements become equivalent pointer-inert real layers, absent source states stay empty, and a color-only request changes no geometry or unmentioned visual value.
- The compiler lifts the core's inline `style` into a lowest-priority `[data-core]` rule so ordinary state declarations override that core; nested visuals remain literal and consume custom properties initialized in Base. Skin paste headers tolerate whitespace and case.
- New skins, conversions, and complete replacements include every canonical paste header, including empty sections that clear stale keyframes or state. The repo-local `skin-author` preflight compiles the current parser/validator, requires the full replacement header set, requires an effective pixel font size and pixel line-height for labeled or `em`-sized cores, and rejects inherited core metrics or any semantic diagnostic before a paste block is returned. Original skins require a painted-footprint check; conversions require source/result rest-and-state geometry comparison because static validation cannot infer visual equivalence.
- The Skin Editor's Button Size section keeps width, height, and sizing behavior in a working preview until an explicit assignment. Each newly selected or replaced working skin starts with Responsive as its pending policy while preserving the placement rectangle. Loading, pasting, or directly editing skin source first renders and measures that working skin at its natural core size; it does not inherit the selected placement's dimensions. Selecting Responsive, Proportional, or Stretch changes only the pending policy and never changes width or height. A width/height edit changes only the working preview, while `Assign Size`, `Assign Size to Panel`, or releasing the blue canvas resize handle is an explicit geometry commit. Responsive constrains `[data-core]` to the exact independently requested host width and height, aligns its authored resting offset to the placement origin, and skips root scaling so authored flexible contents may reflow; fixed nested artwork may overflow and never triggers an automatic fallback. Proportional scales the complete authored root uniformly from its measured natural core and aspect-locks explicit resizing from that natural ratio, even when an older placement box has already been distorted. A ratio-locked skin is therefore proportional-only, not incapable of proportional sizing. Stretch performs explicit independent X/Y root scaling and may distort typography, corners, and effects. An ordinary canvas move changes position only. Render measurements initialize fresh placements and cache natural/visual geometry, but never rewrite an existing placement; the legacy `allowLabelResize` field remains file-compatible without authorizing implicit growth. `Assign Size to Panel` applies the target once to every Button next to that placement on its current Main, Pop, Fan, or other Button surface and compacts that same surface only if it fits. The left rail's `Copy dimensions` captures the focused placement's literal host width, height, and sizing policy; `Add size to selection` remains disabled only until that copy exists or the selection is empty, then releases any legacy uniform-size link and applies the exact box to only the selected placements in one undoable transaction. Existing coordinates remain untouched when the complete visible surface is valid; if a tight row would overlap, neighboring content Buttons shift within the same inferred rows and order using the current Button Sizing gap while the Fan owner stays independent. The action fails atomically only when those preserved rows cannot fit. No other size assignment enables or writes the separate legacy `Same size Buttons` surface format, and no sizing action changes authored skin source. The left rail's Button Sizing field is separate from Button Size: it stores one nonnegative millimeter decimal for both horizontal and vertical automatic spacing, defaults to exactly zero, and does not round through the movement grid. Editing the value alone moves nothing; Reorder, Snap, panel sizing, and automatic Button placement use it when they run. Editor preview viewports scroll-contain literal visual overflow without changing runtime Button overflow. The size preview uses the isolated working skin, so unassigned skin edits still require Assign Skin to Selection or Assign Skin to Panel.
- After optional label injection and host scaling, the transformed `[data-core]` defines the Button's resting measurement and accessible geometry and owns its interaction listeners. The live bounds of the authored interaction target—the core or its optional `data-hit-shape` descendant—are only the broad-phase pointer region. Exact hover, click, double-click, and context interaction use browser hit testing against the core's authored border/clip/SVG shape or that single inner shape. Rounded transparent corners, clipped-out regions, unused core-wrapper space, placement gutters, shadows, glow, and decorative overflow remain inactive; opacity and CSS masks do not redefine the semantic hit shape. Painted descendants inside an SVG hit-shape group participate in the same exact target. Pointer ownership is host-reserved: skin state sections cannot declare `pointer-events`, and inline skin markup may use only `pointer-events:none` on decorative layers. The pointer-inert shadow host permits only that inner shape to receive browser events, and transparent Pop/Fan native gating uses the same target bounds and exact test after physical-to-CSS coordinate conversion. Normal Edit drag and resize sample the complete pointer path and stop at the furthest valid edge without reflowing the workspace with collision warnings. Resize sizing is continuous at one CSS-pixel resolution and does not snap its top-left anchor to the movement grid; ordinary movement retains that grid. The separate undoable Reorder mode pulls the grabbed Button out of reflow so it follows the pointer at animation-frame speed, infers exact visual row membership, exposes every insertion slot in every row plus explicit new-row boundaries, and uses lane-scoped, slot-bounded hysteresis so thin rows and between-row targets cannot be skipped. It closes horizontal gaps within each unchanged row using the current Button Sizing gap, smoothly moves neighboring Buttons out of the way, and normalizes saved placement z-index. A Button never moves to another row unless it is the one being reordered, and an over-wide row fails atomically instead of rebalancing. `Align top-left Button to corner` rigidly translates only selected content until its actual top-left Button reaches the surface corner; a selected Fan owner stays fixed as an independent collision obstacle, and the action changes only `x/y` while preserving row count, membership, indentation, sizes, gaps, order, and z-index. `Align to the Top Left button` keeps that selected content anchor fixed and packs the same rows and within-row order against it using the current Button Sizing gap on both axes; the Fan owner and unselected Buttons stay fixed, and any collision or insufficient surface space fails atomically. The left rail's `Same size Buttons` checkbox persists one authoritative surface size, atomically applies it to every content member, switches them to fixed host sizing with label growth disabled, and preserves their exact x/y coordinates, placement order, and z-index. If the linked size would overlap content Buttons or extend outside the surface, the complete update fails without moving anything. Canvas resizing while linked uses the same size-only transaction; delayed skin/text measurements cannot split the sizes. Unchecking stops future linked resizing while preserving the current fixed layout. The crisp blue Editor outline remains the exact pointer-inert placement/edit frame without an outside glow or badge, not an interaction hitbox, and authored skin source is never rewritten for sizing or hit testing.
- Reorder mode keeps the Button workspace unobstructed by omitting its persistent instruction banner; the overlay appears only when there is an actual blocked-reorder reason. The sidebar no longer exposes a Same size Buttons checkbox. Saved uniform-size surfaces remain compatible; successful Assign Size or Assign Size to Panel releases their legacy size link in the same transaction, while invalid size assignments leave the link and geometry unchanged.
- Button Text wrapping is host-owned: `Shrink` keeps the injected label on one unbroken row, while only `Stack whole words` and `Shrink and stack` may create multiple whole-word rows. Text fit measures the host-injected label glyphs against the constrained core instead of treating the skin's full padded/scroll box as text, and screen measurement discards non-finite painted bounds so preview overflow always remains finite. This policy never rewrites imported skin source or changes `[data-core]` geometry or hit testing.
- Placements retain independent `textFitMode`, `textAlignment`, `textSizeOverride`, `minimumFontSize`, `textOffsetX`, and `textOffsetY`; null text size uses the skin's authored size, while the minimum is only the lower bound used by Shrink and Shrink and Stack. Button Text exposes horizontal `Use skin`, `Left`, `Center`, and `Right` alignment, direct pixel X/Y movement, the placement cycle's logical-state dropdown for editing each state label, and the shared Button Tooltip beside the active label field. The Button Tooltip uses a wrapping multiline editor that grows to its complete content when text, selection, section visibility, or editor width changes; it has no internal clipping or scrollbar, and every character remains visible and selectable. The renderer moves only the host-injected label node: static HTML labels use flow-preserving relative X/Y positioning, already-positioned HTML labels retain their authored positioning model and compose the host offset, and SVG label lines convert the requested screen-pixel vector through the live SVG matrix before applying host-owned positions. This stays valid even when an authored visual state changes the core between inline, block, flex, or grid layout, and regardless of whether `{{label}}` is directly in a face or nested in authored inline markup; it never rewrites skin source, authored transforms, `[data-core]` geometry, or hit testing. Every Button Text change is reflected immediately in its working preview. The single `Apply All` at the bottom of Button Text commits every pending Button Text edit across the editor: each shared Button Tooltip, base labels or placement-cycle state labels, plus each placement's fit, alignment, size, and X/Y position. It preserves state IDs, triggers, visuals, skin source, Button Size, and placement geometry. Static explanatory paragraphs are omitted; concise hover tooltips describe controls. The raw Base, Hover, Play, Pressed, Held, Release, Disabled, and Error code sections remain directly author-editable, including Hover. Skin paste and section edits stay in an isolated Skin Editor working copy until an explicit action. Recognized skin source applies through both native paste and the WebView textarea's normal input path, renders in an always-visible working preview, distributes into the canonical section editors, and clears the transient paste field; unparseable paste remains available for correction. Button Text and Button Color appear first at the top of the Skin Editor, before its toolbar. The toolbar order is `Assign Skin to Selection`, `Assign Skin to Panel`, `Load skin`, `Save skin`, `Save as new skin`, `Update skin file`, and `Open skins folder`; the separate Assign Skin button is removed. Selecting a Button copies its private effective appearance into the isolated editor without associating it with a file. Load opens the native picker directly in `flowcellbackend/local/Button editor/Skins` and shows only real `.flowcell-button-skin.txt` files. A successfully loaded, compilable skin is automatically assigned to the selected Buttons through the existing selection-scoped commit, retaining their geometry, labels, and action bindings; canceling or loading invalid source makes no assignment. Save skin and Save as new skin both immediately prompt for a filename rooted in that shared folder and write canonical paste-ready source without changing a Button or creating an unassigned runtime record; Save retains the working identity and Save as new creates a fresh one. Update skin file is the explicit no-prompt overwrite for a file loaded or saved during the current editor session, and Open skins folder reveals the reusable-file folder for cleanup. File paths never enter canonical Button state. Loading a skin and Assign Skin to Selection write the selected placements' `skinOverrideId` plus the pending sizing policy while preserving x, y, width, height, and text settings, and never change `ButtonRecord.defaultSkinId`; an edited shared source, including the document-wide default skin, is forked first so other placements do not change. Assign Skin to Selection uses the same isolated working skin and scoped commit for only the visible selected placements; Assign Skin to Panel targets every Button on the focused placement's current surface, matching Assign Size to Panel across Main, Pop, Fan, and tool-set Pop occurrences. Both preserve Button identity, text, action bindings, geometry, row structure, and imported skin source. Skin assignments are scoped canonical commits that retain unsaved arrangement geometry and activation cycles; portable file saves remain outside canonical Button state. Pasted recognized skin payloads use validated named-section semantics; there are no Apply Named Sections or Replace Entire Skin buttons. Before changing shared skin source, the Editor resolves inherited `defaultSkinId` use and keeps the safe default as a focused-placement override; a global or panel-wide assignment requires an explicit action and its wider blast radius is visible first.
- Placements may carry an optional `textGlow` of `hover`, `active`, or `always` (absent or null is off), edited in Button Text's Glow Light section and saved by Apply All, Save Settings, settings files, and themes; it never touches skin source. `skins/buttonTextGlow.ts` owns the host-rendered trail, the whole effect: a layer outside the skin's clip, filtered from a hidden copy of the label or symbol, covers the object, rises above it, and fades along the object's top outline. Optional `textGlowCurve` sets strength from that top edge to the trail's end, defaulting to full then nothing, and saves with `textGlow`; the section edits it with `ButtonGradientCurveEditor`, a port of the Theme Editor's curve editor sharing `theme/programPopoutGradientCurve.ts`, and a curve at zero removes the layer. The trail uses the exact painted color. The fade runs on the skin host so the visual latch never waits on it, and an unlit glow adds no measured overflow.
- Directly below Button Text, Button Color begins with a required semantic Color Profile Preview: each material uses one reserved Base seed `--flowcell-button-color-<role>` and deterministic perceptual `--flowcell-button-shade-<role>-<name>` formulas that reference that seed for coordinated face/state shades; labeled skins always expose an independently consumed `--flowcell-button-color-text`; shadow, glow, drop-shadow, and SVG filter/flood occurrences are shown as excluded and preserved per occurrence; ambiguous uses stop for confirmation instead of being guessed. Editable material and Text rows retain the Blender Theme swatch, hex, and eyedropper/native-picker controls; materials preserve authored alpha and Text commits opaque colors. A skin-owned `Highlight on hover` checkbox follows the color rows and writes explicit `--flowcell-button-highlight-on-hover: 1|0` Base data. The renderer uses that setting to enable the host hover highlight; its placement-owned Theme amount defaults to 75% and can scale from 0 through 1000% while preserving `[data-core]` measurement and hit testing. Load skin, Assign Skin to Selection, Assign Skin to Panel, Save skin, and Save as new skin carry the choice with the skin. Older placement-owned highlight values remain a compatibility fallback only when the active skin has no explicit setting. A sibling `Highlight when active` checkbox writes explicit `--flowcell-button-highlight-on-active: 1|0` Base data and defaults off; the renderer uses it to enable the independently scaled host highlight on the latched selected state, so a Tool Set child whose live fields match its authored choice and a selected Main Page Button both read as the active choice without depending on how visible their authored Pressed section is. It has no placement fallback, hover brightening stacks on top of it, edit-mode selection never triggers it, and it changes neither `[data-core]` measurement nor hit testing. The preview stays outside the clean canonical paste block, and each profile edit changes only its isolated working Base root without altering effects, geometry, hit testing, or Assign/Save scope.
- Visual overflow and active visual state are measured separately from the interactive core. Pop/Fan hover, press, hold, and play paint through the resting semantic frame's visible overflow without resizing or repositioning that frame, so transient skin motion cannot make the Button arrangement flicker or move. The authored skin and fixed native host bounds remain unchanged while DOM and native input follow the same live authored interaction shape outside an active press; after pointer-down begins on that shape, native input remains enabled through pointer-up even if transient motion moves the core.
- A mounted skin's shadow DOM is stable: it remounts only when the compiled skin identity (skin id + source fingerprint) changes. Rendered state-label and text-fit changes update the existing label node and rerun fitting in place, so cycling labels never replace `[data-core]` during input. Text offsets are reapplied across visual-transition frames so state-authored translation or SVG scale cannot stale their composed screen-pixel position. Parent re-renders, re-cloned documents, recreated callbacks, and placement resizes never rebuild the skin (a rebuild restarts every skin animation). Host pointer/keyboard listeners likewise stay attached across re-renders, and transparent Pop/Fan windows synthesize the `pointerleave` a cursor-ignoring window would otherwise never deliver.
- For an unchanged compiled skin identity, the skin renderer applies state attributes, cycle labels, placement hover brightness, and visual-measurement state as one latched presentation. A presentation with running finite CSS transitions or animations remains intact until that motion finishes, while later hover/press/hold/release/cycle requests collapse to the newest desired presentation. Raw pointer hover/press attributes, native `[data-core]` hit testing, and backend action dispatch remain immediate; infinite-only motion is nonblocking, and another activation does not restart or queue an active Play visual. A skin ID or source-fingerprint change resets the latch so new compiled source can mount. This host-owned behavior does not rewrite skin source, resize `[data-core]`, or change native coordinate mapping.
- Skin source, validation, compilation, and rendering live only under `FlowCellFrontend/src/button/skins/`; there is no separate skin registry.

Program And Bridge Runtime
--------------------------
- Startup preflight reads `Programs/*/flowcell.program.json` but validates immutable catalog/support directories, declared runner adapter files, mutable roots, and refreshed bindings only for programs already registered in `flowcellbackend/local/bindings.ini`; an available package alone has no runtime presence.
- Fusion 360 is a manifest-owned `fusion-bridge` program. Add Program deploys its startup add-in and bundled Align, Rotate, and Scale Tool Sets; each installed owner gets one constrained registry action and managed Python copy. Its catalog also holds the internal `Import Illustrator SVG` action that Illustrator's Send SVG to Fusion registers on demand; it is not a bundled panel Button. Requests target a ready per-PID bridge directory, the watcher only fires a custom event, and registry resolution, action loading, and all Fusion API mutations occur on Fusion's main thread; the add-in (1.0.1) retries its atomic response replace when a reader briefly holds the file. Align (1.1.0) mirrors Blender Align: X/Y/Z rows of Min | Center | Max | Surface | Origin plus All | X-Y | Group; the last selection is the fixed reference (Align 1.1.1: a selected face, edge, or vertex stands for its whole body, several picks on one body count once at its last pick, and the original picks are reselected afterwards), Shift aligns to the world origin and moves the whole selection, Alt with Center/All/X-Y aligns component origins, Surface and Origin cross to the opposite reference face on every click, and Group moves the selection by one combined-bounds delta. Rotate (1.1.0) keeps Geometry/Origin/World/Pick/Object pivots and adds Distribute, which keeps the selection and adds evenly spaced copies around the pivot (Copy/Paste Body plus Move for bodies, new instances of the same component for components). Both accept root bodies, bodies inside components (Move features in the owning component), and components. Component moves release grounded-to-parent occurrences and capture a position snapshot, because Fusion reverts uncaptured occurrence transforms on the next recompute; pending positions are captured before any feature is added. Bodies have no origin, so Origin modes use their bounding-box center. Scale (1.2.0) mirrors Blender Smart Axis: Base stores the selected bodies' bounds (design attributes), X/Y/Z cycle the locked side (none/center, X- Min, X+ Max) through placement activation cycles synced from every response's `modes`, and Live (on) updates Fusion while dragging the X/Y/Z percentage sliders (off: the drag applies on release). Link X/Y/Z toggle Buttons choose which sliders move together. Each drag creates one non-uniform Scale feature plus one Move feature that lands locked sides on the stored Base edge (or the current edge), edited live and deleted when the drag ends at 100%; typed sizes resize exactly. Fusion children use the Blender tool sets' skins. Popouts opened from FlowCell now raise to the top of the normal window band (Normal placement adds HWND_TOP), a popout with no saved position opened while Main is minimized centers on the monitor under the cursor, and Pop on a panel with only tool sets opens those tool sets instead of erroring. Tool-set field state now merges patches onto the live values: ButtonSurface.applyFieldPatch merges each patch instead of adopting the caller's start-time snapshot, the popout renderer merges only an execution's fieldPatch, and slider results report the latest local values, so a slow program reply can no longer undo a Link toggle or drag a slider backward. An active slider drag marks its input `data-button-field-dragging`, which keeps the transparent Pop clickable until release even when the pointer leaves the slider strip, and the open-time tool-set state query also resyncs result-mapped Button states (e.g. Scale's X-/X+ labels and Live).
- Temp Shots uses `flowcellbackend/helpers/TempShotsCapture.ahk` in the resident backend: it freezes the virtual desktop into memory before showing its rectangle selector, then writes the selected region once to the configured Temp Shots folder and copies that same image to the clipboard as PNG and standard CF_DIB image data for pasting. Escape discards the in-memory capture without saving or replacing the clipboard. No native snipping launch, intermediate image file, duplicate image, recycling, historical cleanup, or clipboard watcher is involved; ordinary Windows screenshot saving stays unchanged. The installed PowerShell launcher preserves folder setup and relays panel requests to the same resident capture used by the hotkey.
- Windows Utility includes a separate Snapshots Button. It uses the Temp Shots rectangle selector, then retains the chosen physical desktop coordinates after the selector closes. Each Space press captures fresh pixels in that box to a `snapshots` folder beside the configured Temp Shots folder and copies the image to the clipboard; Escape ends the session. Snapshots uses the same resident capture backend and folder setup, and does not change Temp Shots single-capture behavior. The shared PowerShell relay locates the resident receiver by its `AutoHotkeyGUI` class and exact title; title-only lookup fails on this Windows runtime.
- Regular Windows `Snipping Tool Overlay` Escape still uses the separate overlay-scoped UI Automation `CloseButton` fallback; Temp Shots cancellation belongs to its own selector and does not depend on a Snipping Tool window name.
- Each registered program selects its transport through `flowcell.program.json`; runner code may know that transport but never owns a pre-baked script or Button inventory.
- The Windows script runner keeps canonical ownership validation intact, then converts Rust's extended-length (`\\?\`) source spelling to the equivalent ordinary DOS/UNC path before launching child processes so Windows PowerShell 5.1 can resolve `$PSScriptRoot` and adjacent package files.
- Catalog execution targets are closed to a small product-neutral allowlist. Page services are declared separately as strict installed-page actions and become reachable only through the validated capability and active identity of an installed owner package.
- Generic installed-page services such as file/folder selection, palette sampling, declared field/package storage, owner state, and generated-Button installation are available only through a currently installed page's validated capability and active identity. Program actions execute the active installed source through its registered runner; Core does not register product compatibility actions, and `flowcell.program.json` has no unused capability-adapter field.
- Save Clipboard Text is a shareable, repeat-installable Windows single-script package: each Button accepts only clipboard text, writes a collision-safe timestamped UTF-8 file, keeps an independent destination in its owner-preserved `runtime/` state, and can reselect that folder while Shift is held (with a user-local fallback for standalone use).
- Toggle Monitors is a repeat-installable Windows Utility package. On its first successful press, each Button opens a native checkbox picker with Group 1 and Group 2 columns for every detected physical monitor; both groups must be nonempty and different, overlap is allowed, and a monitor unchecked in both stays off on both sides. The owner stores schema-3 opaque group identities plus compatible full/reduced snapshot slots in its preserved `runtime/` directory, preselects captured full and selected sides during schema-2 migration only when that state is proven to belong to the owner, preserves configured-but-disconnected selections as annotated picker rows during later edits, and then toggles between those two explicit combinations. Configured members that are disconnected or no longer identifiable are ignored silently and the toggle continues immediately with each group's connected members unless a group becomes empty or both effective groups become identical; degraded layouts never overwrite complete saved snapshots. An unmatched live topology is never captured over either group, saved snapshot contents must match the configured connected identities before any apply, duplicate monitor names remain distinct, configuration fingerprints retire obsolete wireless-drop watchers, cross-Button Group 2 ownership is coordinated, failed switches restore the complete pre-switch snapshot, and a group containing inactive monitors uses Windows best-mode generation with SDC_USE_SUPPLIED_DISPLAY_CONFIG plus SDC_ALLOW_CHANGES, without saving it. The inactive-path fallback must never use SDC_TOPOLOGY_SUPPLIED: selecting a persisted topology is not the documented temporary-mode operation. Both arbitrary groups and failure rollbacks use temporary configuration calls, including the first toggle after boot when old adapter IDs invalidate saved snapshots. Startup/wake safety is independent of both toggle groups: the hidden current-user guardian freshly discovers every Windows-available target, including inactive, wireless, unconfigured, and dummy monitors, deduplicates identities, restores the complete set, verifies it, and persists the stable result. One available monitor is sufficient, even a dummy; there is no real-monitor allowlist, dummy blacklist, or configured monitor-count requirement. Button settings and saved toggle snapshots remain unchanged. If no target is available, it leaves the display topology/database unchanged and retries without warning dialogs. The shared path selector permits wireless targets only for this all-display recovery; ordinary two-group toggles keep their existing behavior. The guardian starts at sign-in and listens for resume plus session lock/unlock; it cannot execute before sign-in, and Windows can deny changes on the locked desktop. Pre-login protection relies on preserving the all-display persistence database with temporary toggle calls. The all-display revision passes 49 tests covering one monitor, dummy-only, unconfigured/missing-config targets, multiple displays, wireless paths, stability, and rollback. Physical reboot/login and sleep/wake remain unverified.
- September 13 monitor regression recovery: the all-display best-mode fallback flattened the saved arrangement and left app windows on the dummy. Automatic recovery is now stopped and DISABLED on this machine pending a layout-preserving implementation. The installer preserves an existing disabled task during routine Button refresh and even with -StartNow; re-enabling requires an explicit task-state change. Restored and persisted the intact second owner's original four-monitor geometry (Odyssey 0,0; Dell 2560,-348; ASUS 6400,-350; CD220 640,1440) using current Windows paths/modes, temporarily disabled the dummy, and repaired the migrated Button's Group 1 cache without changing its group selections. Before-state and original-layout backups are under %LOCALAPPDATA%/FlowCell/ToggleMonitorsStartupSafety/recovery-2026-09-13. Native active/database geometry checks and 50 tests passed; Settings and Photos were brought forward with computer-use. Do not claim the all-display startup feature is currently enabled or layout-safe; real reboot/sleep and future window reopening remain unverified.
- Codex Micro is a catalog-only set of closed Windows script packages. Its independently installable entries use the installed Codex desktop package's exact AppsFolder identity and coordinate-free UI Automation controls for verified task, dictation, navigation, stop, reasoning, and read-only status actions; dynamic numbered slots and unverified approval commands are documented but intentionally have no runnable entries.
- Blender installs require a top-level `run_flowcell_action(context=None, data=None)`. Deployment generates an owner-scoped bridge action from the installed Local source. Selecting the exact root-level entry declared beside `flowcell.toolset.json` now promotes and installs the complete Tool Set package; sibling companions and roots containing both Script and Tool Set manifests fail closed instead of producing partial or ambiguous owners.
- Blender Rename Selected allows only one asynchronous Windows PowerShell/WPF prompt at a time, waits for that process to finish writing its result, then redispatches the confirmed names through the registered owner as a fresh Blender undo step instead of retaining the original context or reloading the live bridge; it does not depend on Blender's optional Tkinter runtime.
- Blender Collections Restore accepts only selections entirely within Snapshots, Trash, or Archive; a Live or unrelated selection cancels before changing object, mesh, or collection data. Its owner action executes the restore directly inside the existing single Blender undo boundary and never reloads the live bridge during that undo transaction.
- Mutating Blender bridge actions pass Blender's required Python operator undo flag as the second positional `True` argument into the shared `REGISTER`/`UNDO` wrapper. The wrapper now records the complete FlowCell mutation as one real Blender undo step instead of suppressing that step and letting Ctrl+Z cross unrecorded object, mesh, or collection changes.
- Named Blender button events execute their declared bridge actions. A declared `pressDown` owns activation instead of also firing the normal release click; paired `pressDown`/`pressUp` events run in order and synthesize their declared hover session for keyboard use. Cycle Collection captures its private visibility baseline on hover, shows one object while held, restores object plus collection-layer visibility on release, and clears the private snapshot on leave without touching the manual baseline.
- The installed Blender Alignment Tool Set normalizes every child slot into an explicit axis/mode command. The active object remains the reference target, other selected objects move, and Origin slots use each moved object's origin as the source while still targeting the active object's bounds center. Every slot measures bounds from each object's depsgraph-evaluated geometry, so modifier results, curves, and text align to the surface Blender actually displays rather than to the unevaluated base-mesh or control-point box; evaluated geometry that collapses to a point falls back to the object's own bounds. Surface slots flip the moved object to the opposite side of the active object on each press and land its facing bound flush against the active bound with no gap. Its `X+Y` child centers each moved object on the active object's evaluated X/Y bounds center while preserving that moved object's Z translation; `Center Everything` continues to center all three axes.
- The Blender Boolean Tool Set replaces its three solver Buttons with one `solver_cycle` Button whose package-owned `cycle_solver` command advances the Blender solver Exact -> Float -> Manifold; its placement activation cycle labels those three states and maps each authoritative `solver` response to the exact index. Its I/U/D children patch one hidden `operation` field and SI/HT/HC/S toggle one hidden Boolean field each, so the selected operation and every enabled toggle stay latched in the skin's authored pressed visual. Every Boolean response also returns a `fieldPatch` of the live Blender scene state, so the latched Buttons and cycled solver label resynchronize from Blender rather than from local guesses.
- The Blender Remesh Tool Set replaces its four mode Buttons with one `mode_cycle` Button whose package-owned `cycle_mode` command advances Voxel -> Smooth -> Sharp -> Blocks, labeled by its placement activation cycle from the authoritative `mode` response. Its six numeric settings are inline-edit Buttons over hidden number fields instead of separate rendered field boxes, so the tool set is Buttons only; Create and Apply carry those live values in their payload templates and the installed source clamps and applies them before the modifier runs. SS and RD toggle one hidden Boolean field each and stay latched while enabled.
- Blender actions that remove or replace Blender data capture response metadata before invalidating the corresponding RNA references; Split strips Blender's generated `.###` suffix and names results `<source> 1` through `<source> N`.
- Blender request/response capabilities remove bridge-owned `id`, `status`, and `display` envelope fields before strict installed-page schema validation while preserving the action's logical response. Blender OBJ Import resolves an explicit 3D View context before opening its modal file picker, retains the registered operator while that picker is active, and rejects duplicate opens instead of replacing an active modal class.
- Blender bridge/add-on deployment changes require reloading the FlowCell add-on or restarting Blender before runtime verification.
- Base Blender add-on repair preserves the installed owner-action registry and `ManagedActions` directory instead of replacing them with empty bundle templates. Blender owner install/sync reads UTF-8 source and registry files explicitly, passes each runtime wrapper its installed source-package root, and validates registration without executing program actions. Bulk recovery can register sources with `Install-BlenderFlowCellButtons.ps1 -SkipSync`, then normalize the completed registry once.
- Tool-set child payloads merge manifest defaults, child defaults, and live Button field/behavior values; child slots are validated against the active record. A child hotkey therefore carries the same current field state, state-only patches, file activation, payload template, and core action as activating that mounted child onscreen. On Run surfaces, children with `fieldPatch` or `toggleFields` automatically use the skin's authored pressed state when their declared values match the live fields, so fixed patches can form radio groups while a toggled choice that clears its peers can also return the group to no selection. A state-only `inlineEditField` child edits one hidden number/text value inside the real Button's injected label node, preserving its canonical placement, skin, `[data-core]`, movement, resizing, and authored animation; pointer-down anywhere on that core explicitly focuses the native popout before refocusing/selecting one stable editor node through pressed/release rerenders. Pressing another Button synchronously commits the active inline editor before that Button dispatches, and a separate `activationPatch` can reset values without affecting selected-state matching. A state-only `selectField` child similarly presents one hidden select field as one canonical skinned Button: the current option becomes its only label, a same-skin transient option fanout opens by hover or pinned click with full keyboard/Escape control, and selecting an option patches field state without creating extra canonical child records. Empty selector IDs and malformed or duplicate option identities/values fail closed rather than falling through to execution. Blender Flatten/Revolve uses that contract for World/Y/Z defaults and uses three inline number Buttons for 360 degrees, 128 steps, and a 0.0001 merge distance; its tracked and installed Python entry is self-contained apart from the generic Blender bridge. Illustrator Ill Align keeps independent X/Y Origin toggles for Min/Max: Origin aligns the selected artwork's center-point origin to the requested anchor edge, while the default performs ordinary matching-edge alignment and Center always aligns centers. Each Surface Button is an immediate geometry-driven action: it reads the current visible-bounds side and moves the artwork flush to the opposite outside anchor surface, so successive presses alternate Min/Max even after manual moves or selection changes. Group mode applies one combined-bounds delta while non-group artwork flips independently. Its command-file actions use the nonblocking persistent Illustrator bridge. Illustrator Rotate treats Art and Anchor as selectable pivot modes for subsequent Positive/Negative actions, accepts both current and legacy pivot payload names, and uses the nonblocking Illustrator process runner. Its original movable 30/45/90 instant-angle Buttons remain separate actions that force Transform and preserve their established Illustrator direction. A movable, skin-authored inline Value Button sits alongside them: Transform resets it to 90 and Distribute resets it to 3, while either mode accepts a directly typed replacement that Positive or Negative commits before execution. An eleventh Copy toggle is squeezed beside Positive; enabling it switches to Transform, selecting Distribute clears it, and Transform then duplicates the selection before rotating only the duplicates while preserving the originals and selecting the new copies for chained presses. Copy also applies to the 30/45/90 presets. Blender Rotate follows the same single-inline-value contract: Transform resets its number-only Button to 15 degrees, Distribute resets it to 3 total positions, and presets plus Positive/Negative map that one current value into the existing Blender payload. Its installed Python action explicitly coerces Negative to a negative angle and Positive to a positive angle before either Transform or Distribute runs.
- Tool-set layouts may declare visible `range` fields (native sliders with required minimum/maximum, optional step and a `{value}` readout `format`). `linkToggleField` names a toggle field; while it is on, dragging one slider moves every range field sharing that toggle by the same amount, clamped so each stays in range. `serviceTrigger: "input"` (range only) also calls the field service while dragging: calls are serialized per field, drag steps coalesce to the latest value, the release always follows as `change`, the payload carries `fieldEvent: { fieldId, trigger }`, and program patches for the dragged link group are ignored until the release response. A field `serviceTarget` of `{ kind: "tool-set-action", command }` with no programName/panelName/ownerFileName targets its own tool set; install, update, and Editor creation fill the routing from the owner's children, and the command must name a declared child slot (its `payload` may override the child's command).
- Blender Tri Poly keeps its six one-click shape actions and exposes the angled-triangle and polygon parameters as two canonical skinned inline-value Buttons showing `50` and `15`. The underlying Angle and Sides fields are hidden, the adjacent `Angle` and `Create` actions consume their live values, and selecting the package's declared `tri poly.py` entry imports the complete self-contained Tool Set.
- Illustrator Ill Align keeps Group as one hidden Boolean state field. Only the existing Group Button exposes that state, toggling between its ordinary authored pressed and non-pressed visuals; no separate field control is rendered.
- Illustrator Snapshot prioritizes the current native artwork selection even when Layer Tree rows are highlighted. It preserves the original Live branch state, makes every layer beneath `Snapshots` visible, and hides only the top-level `Snapshots` root so one eye click reveals the complete stored tree.
- Layer Tree row clicks set the matching native `doc.activeLayer` without replacing Illustrator's artwork selection or creating a temporary document object; its target controls remain the explicit artwork-selection path.
- Illustrator Files exposes `Send SVG to Blender` plus the dependent `Ill Orca` and `Send SVG to Fusion` packages (the Fusion import is described in the 2026-10-24 entry). Both Blender workflows start from Illustrator's current artwork selection, group selected objects by their owning sublayer, expand supported live vector appearance only in temporary documents, discard only proven non-rendering unfilled one-anchor stroke artifacts from those disposable copies, outline text, explicitly select every remaining visible stroked path (including nested paths and de-duplicated compound-path parents) for required Outline Stroke/Expand passes, crop each temporary artboard to its artwork, and export one SVG 1.0 file named from the complete sublayer name. Post-export cleanup supplies the SVG 1.0 doctype and removes unresolved Adobe namespace entities before Blender parses the file. A same-named SVG is replaced during save, while two selected sublayers that sanitize to the same filename fail closed rather than overwrite one another. A positive number at the absolute beginning of the sublayer name sets the requested finished thickness in millimeters, with parentheses optional: `14Main stencil` is 14 mm, `2connect` is 2 mm, and `(2.5) Name` is 2.5 mm; a name without a leading number defaults to 1 mm. Each request carries the saved Illustrator document path, the cleaned artwork's physical X/Y bounds, and its sublayer center's offset from the combined selection center in millimeters. The exporter measures each disposable copy both before and after vector cleanup and adds the exact cleanup-induced center shift to that offset, so outlining, artifact removal, or Pathfinder normalization cannot recenter one layer away from the others; after all layers are clean, it re-centers their combined cleaned bounding box on world zero without changing their corrected pairwise placement. The hidden helper resolves the internal managed Blender import action without installing a Blender panel Button, targets the active Blender instance through the normal bridge, or discovers and launches the newest installed Blender when none is running. Blender imports each batch item through its native SVG importer, consolidates that item's curves, fits X and Y to those physical dimensions through the current scene unit scale, restores the batch's relative X/Y layout, calibrates against the evaluated curve thickness so SVG importer scale cannot alter the requested Z value, converts the result to one mesh, verifies the exact full requested thickness, places its base at world Z=0, links it exclusively to the scene's top-level `Live` collection, removes empty importer-created collections, and leaves the imported batch selected. `Ill Orca` delegates to the installed Send SVG owner, then dynamically resolves and invokes the exact active Blender Files `blender.orca` action with fresh data after import succeeds; the existing Orca package remains authoritative for STL generation, first-run setup, executable selection, and launch. If the active Blender file is untitled, Ill Orca walks upward from the Illustrator document to the nearest valid FlowCell project marker, asks Setup Organization only to prepare that existing project's `.blend` destination, saves there under the sanitized Illustrator filename without overwriting, imports, saves the imported result, and only then invokes Orca. It never runs the broad organizer against the live project; an unsaved Illustrator document, missing marker, unsafe destination, or existing same-named `.blend` fails closed. Already-saved Blender files keep their existing path and save behavior. This workflow supplies a longer, per-call Blender response deadline so a fresh user can finish the Orca executable and single-instance prompts without changing the global bridge timeout. Neither workflow embeds a generated owner ID, user path, Blender version, scene unit scale, or Orca executable path, so a new supported Windows installation can resolve its own environment. Raster, placed, and plugin artwork fails before export rather than guessing destructive vector-tracing settings; the user must Image Trace/expand it intentionally first.
- The SVG handoff normalizes each temporary Illustrator copy into closed filled native paths with a required, baked Pathfinder Unite before measuring or exporting it; visible vector clipping masks are automatically baked from the innermost group outward using Pathfinder Crop on the temporary copy. Compound masks remain intact and released masks are restored to the crop group so holes are not turned into solid silhouettes. Unsupported or unresolved live art still fails without modifying the source document; both `Send SVG to Blender` and `Ill Orca` share this path. The Blender importer derives thickness directly from the owning Illustrator layer name: `3 small` means 3 mm, with no prompt. The transport filename is only the fallback when layerName is absent; an extrudeMm field never overrides the name. Before mesh conversion, Blender raises SVG curve tessellation from the usual 12 steps to 16 for smoother printable sidewalls without changing physical dimensions. Blender welds only coincident curve-conversion seams, dissolves only exact zero-area cap triangles whose middle vertex is collinear within single-precision roundoff while retaining every positive-area skinny face, recalculates normals, and rejects open, non-manifold, remaining-degenerate, duplicate, or self-intersecting extrusion meshes. Its BVH check excludes only same-component outer-cap boundary subdivisions where a standard extrusion wall's manifold cap-plane edge lies wholly on one manifold edge of the outer cap triangle; cross-component contacts, cap-interior crossings, and every other overlap remain unsafe. Successful conversion also removes the SVG importer's zero-user Curve data. A failure removes every new Object, Collection, Curve, and Mesh data block, rolls back the complete import batch, and prevents `Ill Orca` from passing broken geometry to the slicer.
- Zero-area SVG cap seams are dissolved one at a time with the remaining topology re-evaluated after every operation. This prevents a thick extrusion in a millimeter-scaled Blender scene from dissolving stale neighboring candidates as one batch and creating overlapping side faces; valid 14 mm and 2 mm meshes still retain the complete fail-closed integrity check.
- A direct runtime Button execution failure reports through the surface result callback and error styling. Pop-out and Fan hosts render that message on their existing runtime-error surface; the shared Button host does not open a synchronous browser alert, which would be undismissable outside a transparent host's selective native hit-test region and would block every Button in that window.
- Illustrator Utility includes the bundled `clip` single-script Button. It uses the topmost selected closed object as the cutter, destructively replaces the selected stroked paths beneath it with editable segments inside that cutter, temporarily applies the document's default fill when the cutter is unfilled and restores its original no-fill state even on failure, preserves each source stroke's group or sublayer, leaves the cutter intact, and selects the surviving segments.
- Illustrator Utility also includes the bundled one-click `vacuum` single-script Button. It operates only on Illustrator's current native PageItem selection and moves objects strictly smaller than 0.1 mm in both visible X and Y into the exact `Trash > vacuum bin` path. Selected groups remain atomic, and direct selections cannot pull apart a compound path or clipping group. It never deletes artwork, never prompts for settings, creates no Trash layers on a no-op, and restores the canonical hidden/locked Trash root after a move.
- The Illustrator Layers Builder panel contains 20 independent single-script owners plus the Layer Tree page Button. Its versioned catalog contributions update installed owners in place without changing IDs or converting the panel to a tool set. Eleven actions use native Illustrator selection or document-global state and need no Layer Tree highlight: Make Layers, Sort, B Lock, B Vis, Set Lock, Set Vis, Empty Sublayers, Empty Trash, Copy Live, Rename, and Archive. Archive copies each distinct layer owning selected Illustrator artwork, including all its contents and sublayers, into the next Archive > [name] > AN entry. It preserves source names, placement, visibility/lock state, and the original active layer; Archive finishes hidden and locked, and other system roots are left unchanged. Copy Live prompts for a name, duplicates only the current Illustrator object selection into the new direct child under `Live` while preserving source sublayer paths beneath it, then makes the original containing sublayers invisible without deleting the source artwork. Targeted and hybrid actions validate exactly one active generic `illustrator.layer-tree` owner and resolve its strict saved keys directly to Layer references, avoiding synthetic artwork selection and its hidden/locked-state corruption. Snapshot may use a nonempty native Illustrator selection when the validated owner has no highlighted rows; after creating `Snapshots > [name] > sN`, it restores the original active layer before hiding the new snapshot branch so Illustrator cannot leave the active snapshot copy visible. 3D instead prefers eligible selected Illustrator artwork, even over a saved highlight, and consults the Layer Tree only as fallback; it creates the next visible/editable `3D > [name] > dN`, hides prior `dN` versions, and changes only the top-level Live root's visibility, preserving the root lock plus every Live descendant visibility and lock state. Sort protects and orders all five system roots, New Sub targets an exact highlighted parent and reaches its naming prompt without invalidation racing away that saved target, Delete Sublayer protects system roots and Archive/Trash contents, and Empty Trash confirms destructive clearing. New Sub and Rename declare completion-wait execution: its bridge work runs off the Tauri main thread, and the standard program-data invalidation is emitted only after their naming prompts close and Illustrator has applied the layer change. The separate ordinary Layer Tree page prompts for a name before creating either a root or child layer and uses in-page pointer gestures for its two drag operations so the installed-page native/file drag-drop barrier remains intact. Pointer-dragging a row body onto another layer (for example Live) moves it inside through the declared `move` capability. Beside the existing target ring, a separate square selects that row's artwork when clicked and carries that row's artwork directly into a destination row when dragged, without requiring an earlier Illustrator selection: an ordinary drop moves the artwork and an Alt-drop copies it. The flattened result preserves the source's mixed artwork/sublayer visual order, source and destination states are restored, and only results that remain visible and unlocked are selected afterward. Rename in both Layer Tree and Layers Builder lists each distinct target layer with its current name and a separate new-name field; blank fields leave those layers unchanged. Layer Tree prefers layers containing selected Illustrator objects and uses highlighted Layer Tree rows when no artwork is selected, while Layers Builder uses the native Illustrator object selection. Duplicate prefers the distinct owning layers of selected Illustrator objects and falls back to highlighted Layer Tree rows when no eligible objects are selected; overlapping parent/child targets are normalized and each entire layer subtree is copied while preserving layer and artwork visibility and lock state, and names copied roots with the next compact trailing number (`name1`, `name2`) instead of a `copy` suffix. Add to Live prefers native selected artwork and falls back to highlighted saved/version layers. It accepts ordinary top-level source layers and 3D dN versions, temporarily opens closed source ancestors for copying, restores their states even on failure, preserves unrelated system roots, and reports errors or already-Live targets instead of silently succeeding. Force Delete removes every highlighted Layer Tree subtree in one action, taking priority over native artwork selection; with no highlighted rows, it removes each distinct layer or sublayer owning selected Illustrator objects. Ordinary Delete retains selected-artwork priority and falls back to highlighted rows when no eligible artwork is selected. They remove each complete resolved subtree. Force Delete dispatches immediately without a confirmation dialog and opens every target and its ancestors before removing any layer, avoiding invalid deleted Illustrator references between batch items; surviving ancestor states are restored afterward. Ordinary Delete refuses closed targets. Program-data invalidation refreshes this page only when the completed source belongs to the Illustrator `Layers Builder` panel; Toolset actions such as Rotate and Ill Align cannot schedule Layer Tree scans. The Illustrator capability adapter normalizes ordinary and Windows extended-length (`\\?\`) paths before enforcing the Button-owned Local Scripts boundary.
- The registered Illustrator runner starts its persistent package-owned bridge in the background and sends both ordinary Button/tool-set actions and request/response page capabilities directly over its named pipe, avoiding per-click AutoHotkey COM setup and per-request PowerShell startup. Before running an installed source it injects the resolved Button-owned path as `FLOWCELL_SCRIPT_PATH`, so directory-backed packages can find same-source companions and their owner-local runtime even when Illustrator reports the application folder through `$.fileName`. Its versioned `v2` pipe and response protocol prevent an older detached bridge from being accepted after an update. The PID file is diagnostic only: pipe responses and the named process mutex determine live ownership, so a stale but live PID cannot suppress startup. FlowCell starts the bridge without launching Illustrator, then a native background watcher prewarms the cached COM proxy as soon as an Illustrator window exists and repeats that prewarm whenever either the Illustrator PID or verified pipe-owner PID changes. Warm state follows the live bridge PID returned by the pipe, so a stale file cannot trigger continuous prewarming. Pipe-busy errors are distinguished from a missing bridge, and the process mutex plus pipe-ownership check makes duplicate bridge launches exit without overwriting ownership state or spinning. A Button that races the one-time background prewarm waits for it rather than failing busy; otherwise ordinary actions receive a nonblocking acceptance response, while capabilities wait on the serialized bridge so post-action refreshes observe completed Illustrator state. Client I/O stops at the protocol response line and uses cancellable overlapped reads with finite request-class deadlines, so a connected bridge cannot hold a FlowCell blocking thread forever; completion-wait actions retain a longer bounded window for interactive Illustrator prompts. The bridge validates its cached COM proxy before use and, when an action reports the specific stale-server `0x800706BA` disconnect, discards that proxy, reacquires the current Illustrator application, and retries once. Ordinary Illustrator script errors are not automatically replayed.
- Illustrator Layers Builder Sort first moves only invisible direct `Live` sublayers through the established Trash structure, leaves visible direct children and nested hidden descendants in place, and then protects and orders the five system roots.
- Program-owned capability behavior and runtime registrations exist only while an exact active installed owner declares them; owner deletion removes those artifacts through the standard source transaction instead of leaving a Core fallback.
- Illustrator bridge logging is best-effort so a transient log-file lock cannot terminate its named-pipe host. A broken pipe while writing an unacknowledged request reconnects through the normal startup path, while a response-side break is not replayed because Illustrator may already have run that action.

Managed Windows And Layouts
---------------------------
- A legacy Fan owner and an exact Pop-out Fan owner are anchors rather than surface content, so each is exempt from the exact surface-bounds, nonnegative-origin, and overlap rules. Either moves to any offset in any direction, including outside its surface and across the Buttons it opens; `Save Fan Settings` commits a legacy Fan anchor, while `Save Pop-out Settings` commits a Pop-out Fan anchor. Every other Button keeps all three rules. The Editor drags an owner without holding it inside the surface or resolving collisions, pads the working area by however far content escapes so it stays visible, and the runtime derives its expanded frame from the surface box unioned with every placement. Saving with no chosen file now reports that instead of returning silently.
- The Button Editor centers the selected surface in its working area while a surface larger than that area still scrolls from its true top-left.
- Every regular or Tool Set Pop-out keeps one `Pop-out` Placement choice and the same `Fan` interaction checkbox. Pop mode shows all content and hides the retained owner; Fan mode creates or reveals that exact owner with the content, focuses the owner for Button Label and Button Tooltip editing, and selects the existing Fan interaction behavior. Repeated concrete Pop-out occurrences collapse to one selector entry, preferring the currently selected surface without deleting the others. The prohibition on Editor Fan construction still applies to legacy standalone Fan surfaces and membership editing; existing legacy Fans remain supported but are not listed as Placement choices.
- Pop-out Fan runtime begins on that exact owner, hover-opens, hover-out collapses while unpinned, lets owner click pin/unpin, and collapses on Escape. The owner can be placed anywhere in any direction, outside or overlapping the child layout; that relative anchor determines where the Tool Set expands. Expanded geometry holds the owner fixed in physical desktop space and derives the surface origin as `ownerOrigin - ownerPlacement.xy * contentScale * effectiveWebViewPixelRatio`; Pop-out view filters the retained owner, and legacy collapsed Tool Sets keep their synthesized-owner fallback. The large authoring board uses tight hitbox window fitting, so unused board space stays transparent and inert.
